# Refreshed four-module static closure and kmod builtin output handling — 2026-11-03

A fresh integrated static audit for Linux `7.0.0-38-generic` passes with no issues. It binds `nvidia.ko` SHA-256 `5a07385bea9730ddf83c5e482a630ee1b5a71a24604b5583be489ab1754b2c40`, `hybrid580_open.ko` `67052f19d331f10f463f8b04dc8746819fbc4040487009fa7a079c97cd2d4d05`, `hybrid580_legacy.ko` `f38e9bf452d4d28c6ac1281eaa5095523daf231a129721db2176a4e8fa2ee02f`, and `nvidia-uvm.ko` `5bac018abdb0a73f18fe456b131d730d23591e3f8b40204a6238b97efd058432`. All **311/311** frontend imports and **331/331** UVM imports resolve with matching CRCs; module exports match each module's pinned `Module.symvers`. The raw build-only report is local and ignored at `build/experiments/hybrid-bundle-static-current-frontend-v1-20261103/report.json`, SHA-256 `3efc47a234a621a4f268ddd5192dddde671a4a0985aca0f600cb633cdc2f3513`.

The UVM build was repeated against the exact current frontend `Module.symvers`; it verifies **78/78** unchanged direct public API imports and the control-FD dispatch replacement. The static closure also accounts for the hybrid frontend-owned `nvUvmInterfaceDupMemoryWithControlFd` and `nvUvmInterfaceGetGpuProvider` imports. This proves a compile-time symbol relationship, not successful calls at runtime.

An isolated `depmod -b` and read-only `modprobe --show-depends` run passes on kmod 34.2. The observed plan includes the verified `ecdh_generic` builtin, loadable `ecdsa_generic.ko.zst` pre-softdep, Open and legacy providers, frontend, then UVM. The [path-free receipt](../evidence/r595112-hybrid-bundle-depmod-current-frontend-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `71f877a15daeef90cb9bbd9dac6bee10d8b0790f40411f7fa84115645efb5d4d`, binds the depmod result to the static-audit digest and all four module hashes; its qualification is `isolated-dependency-map-only`.

The first real-output normalization attempt revealed that the receipt tool rejected kmod's valid `builtin ecdh_generic` line. [`summarize_hybrid_bundle_depmod.py`](../tools/summarize_hybrid_bundle_depmod.py) now accepts only that declared builtin when it matches the parsed kmod record, and rejects unknown or undeclared builtin lines. The focused receipt tests pass **13/13**. The first full suite also exposed six source-hash pins that still referenced the prior UVM callback-bridge version; those pins were refreshed, and the six affected test groups pass **34/34**.

The subsequent complete pinned CPU/software suite passes **2,762/2,762** in **183.916 s**, with **416** test inputs and **165** implementation inputs unchanged. The path-free [suite receipt v2](../evidence/software-verification-hybrid-bundle-current-frontend-depmod-v2-20261103.json), SHA-256 `d843f31821652a4fc8970a24b9079b00d43da1decc160ec9e145a50071aebf77`, is qualified `software-suite-only`. The initial failed run and raw reports/logs remain mode-0600 under `evidence/logs/` and are not transfer artifacts.

No candidate module was installed or loaded. This does not prove GPU initialization, CUDA execution, UVM runtime behavior, same-process mixed-generation execution, P2P, stability, or performance.

# R580/R595.112 UVM selector-value compatibility — 2026-11-03

The new test `test_r580_gpu_info_selector_ids_match_pinned_r595112_gv100_ids` compiles the same source-value probe against exact R580.196.07 and R595.112.07 headers. Both compile to `gpuArch=0x140`, `hostClass=0xC36F`, `ceClass=0xC3B5`, and `computeClass=0xC3C0`. R595.112.07's `version.mk`, `ctrl2080mc.h`, and the three Volta class headers are hash-pinned; R580's corresponding headers are pinned as well. The existing HAL closure test requires the matching `VOLTA_CHANNEL_GPFIFO_A`, `VOLTA_DMA_COPY_A`, and GV100 rows in the six selected R595 tables. Together these checks establish a static producer-ID/table-ID match.

The v7 producer audit also verifies both R580 class-list query error paths, allocation failure handling, release of the temporary class list, and the caller's early cleanup on RM failure. Its exact R580.196.07 archive hash is `21914a460f17190c43cffb75f0c35aef957e267bfefea267bc18638beae17084`; the source member is `98e4fcca21a17eb9b52793805f4d3a0f8ab9468a4f7154c0eddc2714b1443722`. The compiled R595.112.07 candidate closes **85** callback references across six GV100 selector tables. The path-free [receipt v3](../evidence/r595112-rm-gpuinfo-to-hal-module-closure-sanitized-v3-20261103.json), SHA-256 `aa647f308097cfc2f87b95301cdc5f401e3f5cccc7cffafd84a2850216a3347f`, reports zero missing or duplicate callback definitions; these are source and symbol-closure checks, not live RM or module-init observations.

The focused R580/R595 ABI tests pass **5/5**, the fail-closed HAL producer/dispatch tests pass **18/18**, and the receipt-sanitizer tests pass **6/6**. The complete pinned CPU/software suite passes **2,745/2,745** in **183.491 s**, with **415** test-source inputs and **164** implementation inputs unchanged. See the path-free [software receipt v6](../evidence/software-verification-hybrid-driver-sanitized-v6-20261103.json), SHA-256 `d658477988f298dafae098f1d87e7e806982c654366f8266e43765317d5fcb48`.

This is static header/source evidence. R580 obtains host and copy-engine class IDs from the live RM class list, so this comparison does not show what a V100 RM returns, whether R595 UVM attaches to a physical GV100, or whether its HAL pointers initialize. Module load, CUDA, UVM behavior, mixed-generation execution, P2P, stability, and performance remain unvalidated by this result.

The same R595.112.07 candidate build log shows the module linking, then BTF generation being skipped because `vmlinux` is unavailable. Its `pahole.sh` wrapper cannot find `pahole` and reports a version mismatch; the exact mode-0600 log is SHA-256 `471db958a5189a168edc83cae48e95f54db5403d8e8c5a97a583698882cdec45`, and the module hash is `dea278c8e2593ebf5054da6ff22622f6c2fd8e4a6c9186973a70222d481f99f5`. This is a build-environment/debug-information limitation; no module runtime was tested.

# R595.112.07 RM-to-UVM class producer boundary and eleven Volta paths — 2026-11-03

Pinned Open GPU Kernel Modules source traces `uvm_gpu.c` → `nvUvmInterfaceGetGpuInfo` → `nv_uvm_interface.c` → `rm_gpu_ops_get_gpu_info` → `nvGpuOpsGetGpuInfo`. The RM query supplies `gpuArch`/`gpuImplementation` through `MC_GET_ARCH_INFO` and obtains `hostClass`/`ceClass` from the RM class list; R595 UVM uses those fields for exact HAL lookups. This establishes the source-level producer/consumer boundary. It does not prove the RM accepts a physical GV100, returns the expected R580 class IDs, or initializes the hybrid candidate.

The v9 candidate-path audit follows all **11** former R580 Volta UVM capability flags into the R595.112.07 source tree. The P2P source scan covers **625** C/H files, finds **zero** producer definitions for `rm_gpu_ops_get_p2p_caps`, and records the known consumer-side ordering/index rules separately from the unknown RM values. The new path-free [source receipt](../evidence/r595112-volta-capability-paths-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `8bd3f32390483337b94a6b08fe4ca5cdad1f514df7dbb5204b911e0f4d4cbceb`, is `ANALYZED_STATICALLY` only. The new sanitizer's five adversarial tests and existing candidate-path/software-manifest tests pass **29/29**. The full suite has not yet been rerun after this increment.

REA/Ghidra 6.8.0/12.1.4/JDK 21.0.13 completed a bounded analysis of `uvm_hal_init_gpu` in the exact project-built R595.112.07 `nvidia-uvm.ko` (Dual MIT/GPL; SHA-256 `7284d09d2f3ddafc24805ce9702d3bc596a3f2919468f79c5d9a9ac586f3cd48`; Linux `7.0.0-38-generic` vermagic). The bounded pseudocode contains references to `gpuArch`, `hostClass`, and `ceClass`; the [sanitized receipt](../evidence/r595112-open-uvm-ghidra-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `972550cce64ab7047997ab4dda18326ddf370dd0af93efe0de88750ec75f0632`, keeps no pseudocode. This only corroborates static selector references in the compiled open module. A separate proprietary-package analysis attempt was stopped after re-reading clause 2.2 of the local driver license; its ignored output is excluded and unused.

The focused receipt/path/manifest tests pass **18/18**. The full CPU/software suite passes **2,676/2,676** in **179.707 s**, with **402** test and **155** implementation inputs unchanged. The path-free [receipt](../evidence/software-verification-r595112-open-uvm-ghidra-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `1da77344fe7be4e7e6e391494e7dad0eeaef9ff9b3b30d5585f6ec5ad8c2bec4`, records only software-suite qualification. These results remain static/source and CPU-test evidence. They do not establish a live RM class response, V100 initialization, UVM migration/fault handling, CUDA execution, P2P, mixed-generation same-process behavior, stability, or performance.

The V100 remains assigned to that VM and is not host-visible. The selected RTX-only probe passed synchronous and asynchronous numeric kernels at sizes **1, 31, 256, 4096, and 65537**, including cleanup. In a separate strict-host-key `nvidia-smi pmon` session, one `probe` process overlapped 13 benchmark-window samples and had nonzero SM in 12 of 12 process rows; peak SM was **99%**, peak memory utilization **8%**. The stock `libcuda.so.595.99.02` and probe/fatbin hashes were pinned by the runner.

The path-free [receipt](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-stock-pmon-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `c7354d2e272b82de48280ab838f0ac83d5de64a793adbfa3963717d3a276dfe1`, qualifies execution on the stock RTX only. The combined acceptance correctly remains incomplete (exit 77) because SM70 is absent. This is not candidate-driver, V100, mixed-generation, UVM, P2P, performance, or cryptographic-attestation evidence. Post-run inventory found the RTX at 0% utilization / 2 MiB and the VM active; no driver, module, VM, PCI, firmware, or boot state changed.

The V100 remains behind PC3's active QEMU guest rather than exposed on the host. A fresh local Tailscale status had no separate guest node. Through a temporary strict-host-key tunnel to the already configured `127.0.0.1:22223` guest forward, the pinned guest ED25519 host fingerprint matched, but the available PC3 SSH identity was rejected for `ubuntu` before a guest command ran. The tunnel was closed. This is an access preflight failure, not a V100 inventory or CUDA test; no guest credential or other PC was accessed.

# Current evidence update — 2026-11-03

## GV100 GET/OVERFLOW register contract — 2026-11-03

The R580.178.04 Volta UVM header and R595.99.02 Volta compatibility overlay both pin `hwref/volta/gv100/dev_fb.h` to SHA-256 `0175a270fa5af9d3a62d7fda2c9e536b58551db6bb1e9c123b84ffeec9a32584`. NVIDIA's published Volta header is byte-identical across the inspected R595.99.02, R595.112.07 (commit `d8257d95ae5fed364163e4df51db66df8b1ee0d2`), and R615.78.08 (commit `1a82fbfda8f321b056eb3568850f9316c5e465c5`) snapshots, SHA-256 `d2590fe14ddf7b2a7d388612c66319017b6bb3db797b7aaabb54b40eb4ee0900`. The audited field contract fixes the GET register stride at 20 bytes, two buffers, PTR at bits 19:0, GETPTR_CORRUPTED at bit 30, and OVERFLOW at bit 31; both status CLEAR values are one.

The v5 source-pinned CPU harness compiles the exact Volta callback and service bodies from the R580 baseline and R595.99.02 compatibility overlay. It passes **244/244** checks and **2/2** test-only release-assert status probes. A mutation regression rejects a changed PTR field. The [path-free register receipt](../evidence/volta-gv100-get-register-cross-branch-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `44f73bdbab86076bad3330e69f414ff071a4208be502a496a531d9feca6dd235`, binds the header/source hashes and private harness outputs.

The complete CPU/software suite passes **2,663/2,663** in **182.913 s**, with **396** test sources and **153** implementation inputs unchanged; see [receipt v2](../evidence/software-verification-test-import-closure-sanitized-v2-20261103.json), SHA-256 `e5e7dcc94f3268638274e8c7b9984e248a6f5a888a98c06f5b10b7bcf3d7553b`. Neither result touches a GPU: MMIO, DMA ordering, physical overflow handling, module load, CUDA runtime, and hardware stability remain unvalidated.

## Host-staged cross-provider transfer fallback contract — 2026-11-02

The current opt-in `--peer-transfer` probe now checks both directions between SM70 and SM120 through separate CUDA contexts using a 4 KiB portable-pinned host buffer. It verifies source device-to-host and host-to-destination device data synchronously and asynchronously, using a stream and synchronization boundary per context, and reports cleanup separately. No foreign device pointer is passed to the opposite backend. This path can validate an application's fallback copy semantics even when direct peer access is unavailable; it does not attest the physical DMA route, NVLink, BAR1, GPUDirect, or direct P2P. `direct_dma_attested` remains false. Direct peer-copy and destination-kernel checks remain separate and may be skipped or fail independently.

## R595.112.07 source-core four-module and kmod static closure — 2026-11-02

The R595.112.07 candidate now uses a locally built open core, rebuilt standard-name frontend, and rebuilt hybrid UVM consumer for Linux `7.0.0-38-generic`. The retained R595.99.02 hybrid UVM patch set **0001–0012** replayed on the pinned R595.112.07 `kernel-open` tree with **12/12** patches applied at zero offset and zero fuzz. The rebuilt frontend `nvidia.ko` has SHA-256 `ae7f2f72e8214b1d5b970e3b2f202c059d82a290f61a99fa07cce24188a58748`. It links to the source-built core `Module.symvers` SHA-256 `46a372fa1607547fd79dad700529f7756050ed1d02b958056da3a9405bb99117` and the R580.196.07 provider. The rebuilt hybrid `nvidia-uvm.ko` has SHA-256 `453b257fdc6f09aa3c67a0a530fd2431ab3dedaa825e6657372a47288dd97048`; 78 direct public imports match, and the control-FD address-space dispatcher matches CRC `0xee6dd59d`.

The path-free [four-module closure receipt](../evidence/r595112-source-core-static-closure-sanitized-v2-20261102.json), SHA-256 `aa0fdba4684aaff5ede0709a0fc8c120e2efaf92dad9d2b45739afc2749d8341`, binds the static symbol audit and isolated kmod dependency/softdep resolution with zero unresolved issues. The detailed path-bearing audit and depmod reports remain local. Fixing the verifier to distinguish the hard dependency graph from softdep targets added an 8-test focused suite; it passes **8/8**.

The complete pinned CPU/software suite passes **2,600/2,600** in **180.131 s**, exit 0; the combined receipt records the detailed suite report hash. The raw log is mode-0600 and remains local. Failed frontend attempts v1 and v2 are retained as private diagnostics; they used respectively a stale R580 provider export table and the wrong UVM ABI reference, and are not the successful build.

REA 6.8.0/Ghidra 12.1.4 statically decompiled the compiled UVM `uvm_ioctl` dispatcher and the custom frontend `nvUvmInterfaceDupAddressSpaceWithControlFd` implementation. The decompilation confirms expected call-path presence and owner/provider guards in the exact module artifacts; it does not verify execution. Ghidra reports unreachable blocks and unresolved local-variable annotations, which remain explicit limitations.

Qualification is **compiled and statically closed** only. No experimental module was installed or loaded, no GPU or remote PC was accessed in this increment, and no CUDA, UVM, P2P, performance, recovery, or endurance result exists for this candidate. Functional equivalence of the replacement core object also remains unproved. A fresh PC3 inventory and reversible hardware canary remain prerequisites for runtime qualification.

The authorized PC3 stock RTX 5080 kernel phase reported CUDA Driver API 13020, SM120, and numeric result `1200/1200`, with successful cleanup and two device-allocation observer snapshots. The raw report is mode-0600 under ignored `evidence/logs/`; this remains stock Blackwell reference evidence, not hybrid or V100 proof.

REA 6.8.0/Ghidra 12.1.4/JDK 21.0.13 analyzed the local GPL Nouveau module `nouveau.ko` without loading it or accessing a GPU. Sanitized [receipt v7](../evidence/nouveau-gv100-static-source-audit-v7-20261102.json), SHA-256 `41c50fad05eb9d8056c9f1f2185775e28e15e7293e3302d798cd222cb51b40d2`, pins the additional call `gp100_vmm_new_ → nvkm_vmm_new_` on the same module hash used by the prior GV100 VMM and join analysis. A separate exploratory build selected the renamed hybrid provider and `nvidia-modeset` together. That configuration is not the intended split-backend pairing: the Blackwell system keeps the stock `nvidia`/modeset stack, while the Volta experiment uses `hybrid580_open`.

Clarification from a controlled rebuild (2026-11-03): the earlier source-Makefile edit was followed by external Kbuild against an already generated `nv-modeset-kernel.o`, so that attempt could not test the compiler flag. The new A/B cleans `src/nvidia-modeset/_out` in each separate copy and reruns the top-level source build before Kbuild; the new patch removes the observed RETHUNK warning class but leaves other objtool warnings unless frame pointers are retained. The four-module inference bundle still does not include modeset, and the reduced renamed-provider link still has the stock `nvidia_get_rm_ops` import unresolved. The earlier patch/test removal refers only to that invalid Kbuild-only attempt; the new source-build patch is a separate experiment documented in the [RETHUNK comparison](REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md#r59511207-modeset-return-thunk-differential--2026-11-03).

Qualification is **compiled**, not installed, loaded, or tested on hardware. No V100, RTX 5080, CUDA, UVM, P2P, same-process execution, or performance test ran in this increment.

# R595.112.07 Linux 7.0 RETHUNK build patch — 2026-11-02

An unmodified, clean NVIDIA Open GPU Kernel Modules checkout at commit
`d8257d95ae5fed364163e4df51db66df8b1ee0d2` built for the installed Linux
`7.0.0-38-generic` headers, but emitted **11,101** objtool warnings of the form
`'naked' return found in MITIGATION_RETHUNK build`. Its `src/nvidia/Makefile`
enabled indirect-branch thunk flags without enabling the matching function
return thunk flag. Linux's x86 build selects
`-mfunction-return=thunk-extern` when `CONFIG_MITIGATION_RETHUNK` is enabled,
and its return-thunk implementation exports `__x86_return_thunk`
([Linux x86 Makefile](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/master/arch/x86/Makefile),
[return thunk](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/master/arch/x86/lib/retpoline.S)).

Added the one-line, x86-64-only patch
[`0001-open-core-rethunk-return-thunk.patch`](../patches/r595.112.07/0001-open-core-rethunk-return-thunk.patch).
It uses NVIDIA's existing `TEST_CC_ARG` support check, so unsupported compilers
do not receive the flag. Exact upstream Makefile and license inputs are retained
under [`tests/fixtures/upstream/nvidia-r595-rethunk/595.112.07/`](../tests/fixtures/upstream/nvidia-r595-rethunk/595.112.07/).
The new regression test verifies the upstream file hash, zero-offset/zero-fuzz
patch replay, the patched file hash, and rejection of modified hunk context.

On the same source commit, kernel headers, GCC 15.2, and local `pahole` 1.31,
the existing source-preparation tool copied the exact checkout and applied the
patch at zero offset and zero fuzz; a clean no-load build of that prepared tree
produced `nvidia.ko` and `nvidia-uvm.ko` for
`7.0.0-38-generic`. The patched build had **zero** RETHUNK warnings and zero
errors; the core object references `__x86_return_thunk`, and modpost completed.
The unpatched comparison had 11,101 RETHUNK warnings, so this controlled source
change resolves that warning class in this build. **Nineteen other compiler
warnings remain**: six `-Woverloaded-virtual`, seven `-Wexpansion-to-defined`,
and six `-Wmissing-prototypes`. BTF generation was skipped because the target
headers did not provide `vmlinux`.

The [sanitized build receipt](../evidence/r595112-rethunk-source-build-v1-20261102.json)
records source, patch, kernel, toolchain and module hashes. The test is in the
pinned CPU suite, which passes **2,590/2,590** tests in **178.251 s** with 388
test inputs and 92 implementation inputs unchanged. An upstream NVIDIA issue describes a related Linux 7.0
objtool failure mode when objtool is bypassed; that report is contextual risk
evidence, not proof about this build
([NVIDIA issue #1095](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/issues/1095)).

Qualification: **compiled**, not loaded or installed. No GPU was accessed; the
R580 Volta provider, GV100 initialization, Blackwell initialization, CUDA,
UVM/P2P runtime, same-process execution, and performance remain unvalidated.
This patch improves the modern open-module build path; it does not certify the
hybrid stack or the separately built R580 provider.

# Latest R580/R595 P2P source/ABI validation — 2026-11-02T17:09Z

The exact MIT source excerpts for R580.196.07 and R595.112.07 show that R580's `UvmGpuP2PCapsParams` ends after its two BAR1 DMA ranges, while R595 appends `NvBool bar1PcieAtomics[2]`. The two pinned `nvtypes.h` files define `NvBool` as `NvU8`. An x86-64 C compiler probe measures the old and new structs at **64** and **72** bytes, with `bar1PcieAtomics` at offset **64**. Source checks also pin the link-label difference: R580 reports generic PCIe after its BAR1 probe; R595 reports `UVM_LINK_TYPE_PCIE_BAR1` when BAR1 is present.

[`audit_p2p_branch_delta.py`](../tools/audit_p2p_branch_delta.py) passes **8/8** source contracts and the compiled layout probe; its [receipt](../evidence/r580-r595-p2p-abi-delta-v1-20261102.json) SHA-256 is `dfd5c33957f54fbe300ae4d9d8d0c41568a71233acb553daa5ce623d8d8a57c8`. The converter test passes **4/4** with added `_Static_assert`s for both struct sizes, shared prefix offset, and the new R595 field offset; the R595 producer audit passes **7/7**. The full CPU/software suite passes **2,576/2,576** in **174.187 s**, exit 0. Its [receipt](../evidence/software-verification-r580-r595-p2p-abi-v1-20261102.json), SHA-256 `53f2639796c948c75ccc7a3660f9715881da0bbfbce646bb30f5495468dc2247`, confirms unchanged test and implementation inputs and `software-suite-only` qualification.

This is static source plus CPU/compiler evidence. It does not establish a live RM response, BAR1 mapping, PCIe atomic behavior, cross-provider UVM, CUDA execution on either GPU, or performance. No module was loaded and no GPU was used.

## Historical software validation — P2P UVM consumer audit — 2026-11-02

The R595.99.02/v15 UVM source audit now pins handle normalization, capability-array copies, per-direction aperture selection, and BAR1 DMA-index use. It scanned 624 candidate C/H files and found the RM capability producer only as an interface declaration/call path, with no source definition. The header's peer-ID and PCIe-atomic direction comments differ; UVM's consumer uses index 0 for lower-ID-to-higher-ID access and index 1 in reverse, but the RM output contract remains unresolved. The private [mode-0600 v9 receipt](../evidence/volta-uvm-r580-r595-v15-capability-paths-v9-20261102.json), SHA-256 `cc543d52606408f4b8f784b198532be180f5b8269e3d78f618a6f2be0a1461ce`, records this as static evidence only. Focused tests pass **17/17**, including four direction/order mutations.

The pinned CPU/software suite passes **2,563/2,563** in **175.856 s**, exit 0, with unchanged hashes across **371 test sources** and **90 implementation inputs.** [Mode-0600 suite receipt](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261102T-p2p-v9.json), SHA-256 `210be7b954e9f42c791ab05828e3e630ae951ff46c81ae041aa8c10229b22aed`; mode-0600 log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261102T-p2p-v9.log`), SHA-256 `58320c6f875983662b307fa9a8faefe290681a0cf9f0e2e83cfe0370ce0e526e`. Qualification is `software-suite-only`, with runtime and module-loading validation false.

Scope correction: the receipt and its missing-definition result scanned the UVM subtree only. The open RM producer is now statically traced in the source-validation entry above; runtime capability values and hardware behavior remain unknown.

NVIDIA's CUDA guide states that Linux bare-metal PCIe P2P is unsupported with IOMMU enabled, while VM passthrough uses IOMMU/VFIO; the NCCL guide warns that ACS can redirect peer traffic through the CPU root complex. These are environment constraints for a future P2P test, not evidence of current PC3 state or a request to change system configuration ([CUDA guide](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/03-advanced/multi-gpu-systems.html#host-iommu-hardware-pci-access-control-services-and-vms), [NCCL guide](https://docs.nvidia.com/deeplearning/nccl/user-guide/docs/troubleshooting/gpu_troubleshooting.html)). No PC or GPU was accessed in this increment; same-process Volta/Blackwell CUDA, P2P, performance, and endurance remain unvalidated.

## REA/Ghidra artifact differential and full software suite — 2026-11-02

REA 6.8.0/Ghidra 12.1.4 analyzed the hash-pinned open R580.196.07 and R595.99.02/v15 UVM modules. Ghidra reconstructs the R595 TLB callback's full 334-byte body; for R580 it truncates the function to the five-byte `__fentry__` call because that thunk is marked no-return. ## CUDA backend-output contract follows the pinned llama.cpp source — 2026-11-02T11:08Z

The exact pinned [`test-backend-ops.cpp`](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/1c5ed702c9e540ac14cc8c78100ea3984bc0e227/tests/test-backend-ops.cpp) prints a backend's numerical test summary within its backend loop. Its `run_fa_vec_slice()` helper returns without printing a summary when the backend does not expose the Metal tuning callbacks; those callbacks are implemented in `ggml-metal`, not the pinned CUDA backend. Requiring the optional `fa_vec` line therefore rejected normal CUDA output. The project validator now requires the nonempty, all-passing numerical summary to be in the selected CUDA backend's output block, accepts the expected no-`fa_vec` CUDA path, and validates a unique nonempty zero-failure `fa_vec` summary if one is actually emitted. CPU regressions cover cross-backend summary leakage, absent/empty/failed optional `fa_vec`, and JSON boolean values masquerading as integer success.

Focused runner/contract/artifact-verifier tests pass **38/38**. The full CPU/software suite passes **2,506/2,506** in **170.963 s**, exit 0; the receipt pins 367 test-source and 85 implementation-source inputs with no changes during the run ([receipt](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261102T110559Z.json), SHA-256 `9720d9ef6f7cd19b77eb0c1e1da238456fe846e17ac7e3f3b0c1f9a547941905`; mode-0600 log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261102T110559Z.log`), SHA-256 `b0eaa32905420787d5ab5a7d8ca9f709cbf08762592a86d48b19b201beafb3a4`). ## R595 external-map duplicate/free cleanup composed with the hybrid RM bridge — 2026-11-02

The pinned [NVIDIA R595.99.02 `uvm_map_external.c`](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/blob/595.99.02/kernel-open/nvidia-uvm/uvm_map_external.c) copies `rmCtrlFd` into `user_rm_mem`, then calls the old `nvUvmInterfaceDupMemory` API without passing that file identity. Patch `0003-uvm-external-allocation-control-fd.patch` changes the duplicate call to the FD-aware entry point. A CPU/UBSan harness now composes that exact patched source statement with the production `nvUvmInterfaceDupMemoryWithControlFd` wrapper and fake RM callbacks.

The same public client/object values route to Open or legacy according to the control FD. A provider mismatch returns `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED` before either RM callback and leaves public outputs zero. An RM error with a partial native handle exposes no public handle or metadata and quarantines the source owner. After a successful duplicate, the harness runs the exact `uvm_release_rm_handle` callsite through the production `nvUvmInterfaceFreeDupedHandle` wrapper, modeling cleanup after a later map-setup error; it verifies that the creating backend releases the handle and the frontend owner record is retired. Replacing the control FD with invalid descriptor 99 in a temporary patch copy makes the composition test fail at owner resolution, as expected.

The project includes a short R595 source excerpt with NVIDIA's license notice and a pinned hash so these contracts still run from a clean Git checkout without ignored `build/research` trees. When the full R595 source is present, tests also verify its hash and apply the patch to the entire file with zero offset and zero fuzz. Focused tests pass **3/3** with both the full source and fixture fallback. The full software suite passes **2,451/2,451** in **173.119 s**, exit 0; all 362 test inputs and 77 implementation inputs were unchanged during that run. See [composition receipt v2](../evidence/r595-external-map-bridge-composition-v2-20261102.json) and [suite receipt](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261102T070602Z.json). The earlier [v1 composition receipt](../evidence/r595-external-map-bridge-composition-v1-20261102.json) is retained as the first duplicate-only slice.

This is CPU source-composition evidence, not the complete UVM unwind: it does not execute `uvm_ext_gpu_map_destroy`, the `nv_kref_put` lifecycle, RM ioctls, or a GPU. No module or driver was installed or loaded, and no GPU was accessed. Same-process V100+Blackwell CUDA, live UVM, P2P, and performance remain unvalidated.

## Prior GT200_DEBUGGER exception-mask route hypothesis — superseded as a runtime conclusion — 2026-11-01T10:17Z

The [static route receipt](../evidence/gt200-debugger-hybrid-control-route-v1-20261101.json), SHA-256 `b521bee737525c869a069565129f02d94926bb58b1b90135e9b5b34ee5664173`, traces the hybrid frontend's fixed exception-mask bridge through the current R595 and R580 provider wrappers and pinned RM sources. Both wrappers enter ordinary NV04_CONTROL; the RM debugger class then forwards the internal command using NV01_NULL_OBJECT. RM serverControl looks up hObject in the client's resource map before dispatch. The inspected client/resource creation paths use nonzero handle ranges and do not map handle zero. The source-derived expected result is NV_ERR_OBJECT_NOT_FOUND; no runtime response has been measured.

This remains a source-path hypothesis only. The later stock V100 reference observed public `0x83DE0309` with RM status 0 but did not resolve its target class; the stock R595 RTX capture resolved `GT200_DEBUGGER` and also succeeded. The universal failure prediction is therefore not established. The current default keeps the normal R580 bridge, while the compiled fail-closed alternative is excluded from the default patch series; see the newer route-disposition section at the top of this file.

Regression receipt [v2](../evidence/hybrid-bundle-r595-uvm-regressions-v2-20261101T1016Z.json), SHA-256 `a643b1bb32441e76be075beb0e67b4939ebfcd5ab9cf908c5dd0f29e972614a0`, passes **93/93**. One test composes the frontend bridge, hash-pinned provider builds, six pinned release-source snapshots, and exact resource-server code. It validates the source-path finding only. No candidate module was installed or loaded; no GPU was accessed.

This remains a source-derived failure prediction, not proof that CUDA context creation fails. The archived context capture had one unresolved debugger-command candidate, but did not identify it as `0x83DE0309`; a supported internal-client/subdevice route has not been identified in the provider adapter interface. The next PC3 context capture must retain the newly summarized exact command and RM status before this can be called a blocker.

## Physical RM firmware, handle, and resource-map dispatch contract — 2026-11-01

Static regression checks across R580.178.04, R595.99.02, and R615.71.10 confirm that `_gpuRmApiControl` first rejects `IS_FW_CLIENT(pGpu)` with `NV_ERR_INVALID_STATE`. On non-firmware clients, its direct-call fast path requires the GPU's internal client and internal subdevice handle. `GT200_DEBUGGER` forwards using its owning client but returns `NV01_NULL_OBJECT` (`0`) as the internal object handle, so that request uses ordinary RM control with the unchanged zero handle. `serverControl` runs `clientGetResourceRef_IMPL` before class dispatch. A CPU fixture compiles and runs that exact lookup function from all three pins: an unmapped handle returns `NV_ERR_OBJECT_NOT_FOUND`; a mapped handle succeeds. The OpenRM dispatch route is blocked unless a zero-valued resource is registered. This does not establish the runtime client type on any PC; no handle-0 registration or hardware dispatch was observed. Proprietary R580 RM behavior remains unknown.

Debugger-dispatch/provider focus passes **8/8**. Fresh full suite v7 passes **2,420/2,420** in **151.454 s**, exit 0. [Receipt](../evidence/software-verification-debugger-route-v7-20261101.json), SHA-256 `fe58f7fad2ff0170b4620b531e89328b03d18ada09060e153003f1df4dd6f945`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-debugger-route-v7-20261101.log`), SHA-256 `1da5b93fa8a0e2adc31235d448c9ddc7d6f651503837225e35db11d522d55423`. Receipt qualification is `software-suite-only`; runtime/hardware validation, module load, and installation are false. PC3 remains off and was not contacted.

The R580 native build revealed two source-configuration defects: an unsupported-backend check was inserted into NV01_DEVICE_0 allocation, and a R595 consumer link-enum conversion was compiled for the native R580 UVM ABI. CPU tests verify that this rejection creates no object and does not quarantine the root.

## Latest observer v38 verification — 2026-11-01

The isolated GT200_DEBUGGER lifecycle observer now emits `object_handle_matches_prior_debugger_allocation`. The field means the numeric `hObject` matched any earlier successful debugger-allocation handle recorded under the same root. It is historical only: after free it may stay true, and numeric handle reuse can produce equality without object identity. The separately resolved target class comes only from the live registry and remains `not-observed-live-object` after free. The field is redacted and does not forward controls.

The observer reproducibly builds with no diagnostics: binary SHA-256 `4c0a35984b4cb944f38a28ae1d2131775f3f6ee0838c3f1c7aa13f8f7cc84b92`, [build receipt](../evidence/gt200-debugger-lifecycle-observer-v5-20261101.json), SHA-256 `452d0cafcba7d9cab4786df95f4dc435a32b7e9c012724c8a2c3595eb261831e`. ## Mixed-provider P2P guard and full test-input provenance — 2026-11-01

A host fixture extracts and compiles the production `nvUvmInterfaceGetP2PCaps` and `nvUvmInterfaceP2pObjectCreate` callbacks with the pinned R595.99.02 `get_parent_p2p_caps` and `parent_peers_discover_static_link` functions. They do not establish that the physical V100/RTX 5080 topology lacks P2P, that a driver reports real peer capabilities correctly, or that DMA, UVM migration, or cross-provider CUDA works. ## GT200_DEBUGGER lifecycle observer and acceptance wiring — 2026-11-01

Observer schema v35 adds a bounded lifecycle mode for a later PC3 capture. It preserves only fixed `GT200_DEBUGGER` allocation payload fields, frees whose live class resolves under the same observed RM root, and fixed `NVOS54` headers for controls targeting a uniquely resolved live debugger object. The selected control `hClient` must resolve to exactly one root before `hObject` class lookup. No nested control parameter is read; raw handles and addresses remain excluded. Unresolved free classes and debugger-command candidates have separate counters. Allocation payload snapshots are counted independently, so an empty event capture is a valid observation and does not imply that no event exists outside the capture window.

The isolated context capture is exposed through `tests/run_hybrid_acceptance.py --gt200-debugger-lifecycle` and `tools/run_remote_selected_gpu.py --gt200-debugger-lifecycle-capture`. Both validate v35 metadata and per-record redaction; the remote transport independently revalidates the child report and its summary. CPU-only fixtures cover zero events, alloc/free/control records including status transitions, and malformed raw-handle rejection. Focused tests pass **176/176**; the complete repository suite passes **2,371/2,371** ([full-suite receipt](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261101T035214Z.json), SHA-256 `d075825dc731329c2aa32b00033f1682b4b0ed5e4b86c17d10d61f49e4d5f576`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261101T035214Z.log`), SHA-256 `e80413acdeaf923dae752b9befb2cca988c2ae17ff1ccdcd50aa0018fde9ff83`). ## PC3 read-only inventory and RTX 5080 stock CUDA control — 2026-11-01

PC3 was reached using its pinned host key and a PC3-only SSH identity. A fresh `sudo -n true` check still reports `interactive authentication is required`; the privileged inventory failed without writing a snapshot, and no alternate PC was contacted. The bounded unprivileged collector records the host GPU, loaded NVIDIA modules, process table, VM service, and QEMU process in exclusive mode-0600 evidence. Its initial [inventory](../evidence/pc3-user-gpu-inventory-v1-20261101T0223Z.json), SHA-256 `1481c1789154e0173d26fe5e7c7f49f402ce4c60d2eba8849c8c0c2cb48ed720`, records RTX 5080 `0000:02:00.0`, R595.99.02, and V100 `0000:81:00.0` attached to `vfio-pci` while the QEMU service is active. It does not establish the guest's driver or CUDA state. A PC3-only read-only probe then found QEMU's localhost SSH forward to the guest and matched the expected ED25519 host-key fingerprint, but the available PC3 key was rejected (`Permission denied (publickey)`), so no guest command executed. Evidence: [guest SSH access probe](../evidence/pc3-v100-guest-ssh-access-probe-v1-20261101T024717Z.json), SHA-256 `73ff39b2ac8ee0789ecd548658bc26b6e935b52a558ba6186a3dedf4f7fc33e8`.

The pinned `probe-peer-transfer-v3` bundle ran against that RTX 5080 with exact system `libcuda.so.595.99.02` hash `192d4eedb1b39f0568f2b5423d35227d13b32b9025599e1968a9081982da207d`. The captured report says SM120 was targeted, synchronous and asynchronous numeric kernels passed at all five sizes (1, 31, 256, 4096, 65537), and cleanup passed. Its report contract is valid, but final acceptance is `incomplete-hardware-missing` with exit 77 because the probe's required set includes SM70, which is not visible on the host. The runner deliberately leaves `cuda_execution_validated=false` because its result is not independently attested. The run was not a benchmark. Its temporary remote workspace was removed, and the fresh post-run inventory found no running GPU processes. Full [RTX control report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-stock-reference-v1-20261101T0222Z.json), SHA-256 `956eed608d062eae5d19d5c31b55f5b9b1317df765a47175cd2f85d9c30f3572`.

A separate stock microbenchmark is preserved as a **single-run diagnostic**, not added to the multi-run aggregate. It used nine launch batches (256 empty-kernel launches per batch) and seven pinned-copy samples per transfer size with data-integrity checks. On this capture, device-event launch median/p95 is **1.262/1.304 µs**; host submission wall median/p95 is **1.262/1.285 µs**. At 64 MiB, median pinned H→D is **51.114 GB/s** and D→H **23.450 GB/s**. The graph benchmark and every transfer-size integrity check passed. The final report still exits 77 for absent SM70 and sets `cuda_execution_validated=false`. No clocks were locked, and there is no hybrid comparison or gain claim. [Raw report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-stock-performance-baseline-v1-20261101T0226Z.json), SHA-256 `169595669bc2ab3f02d0d8dc3da944bf2c4adc160083a0f1b83a12b04db63154`.

Separate isolated stock runs also report two CUDA Graph replays passing and managed allocation plus prefetch passing on the RTX 5080; both retain `missing_sm=[70]` and the non-attested qualification. [Graph report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-stock-cudagraphs-v1-20261101T0227Z.json), SHA-256 `63195de06ade71fc7763e5cab428b71b8510c27dcde01cc06d7caf1074577e3c`; [managed-memory report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-stock-managed-memory-v1-20261101T0228Z.json), SHA-256 `567390f4db8e90a310b474d80630f65f49705af339fa7fe04468331e2fee5088`.

After PC3 returned, three sequential benchmark captures used the same pinned probe/fatbin and selected stock RTX 5080. The median of the three run medians is **1.274 µs** device empty-kernel launch (range **1.269–1.283 µs**), **1.270 µs** host submission (**1.266–1.271 µs**), **51.131 GB/s** H→D (**50.934–51.173**) and **23.377 GB/s** D→H (**23.305–23.507**) at 64 MiB. Each report records nine 256-launch batches and seven pinned-copy samples per size with integrity checks. The per-device kernel and copy fields pass, but every overall run exits 77 because SM70 is absent and keeps `cuda_execution_validated=false`; these are non-attested stock diagnostics, not hybrid validation or a gain claim. No clocks were locked or inference load measured. [Run 1](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-stock-performance-repeat-v1-20261101T0240Z.json), SHA-256 `0aec6fa39f252a6614d9c8217a59d7c079c4a7858f62c20dae8851e4816c5acb`; [run 2](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-stock-performance-repeat-v1-20261101T0241Z.json), SHA-256 `36d962b3f67b34a049d7e38d0c4970d8e77ebfcca1b08e04bee3d0842727aae1`; [run 3](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-stock-performance-repeat-v1-20261101T0242Z.json), SHA-256 `354ddfca50801d7624c95cb77c252501b1cd58b6fa9b870c7fbabe358eaf4ae7`.

The post-benchmark [02:40Z inventory](../evidence/pc3-user-gpu-inventory-v1-20261101T0240Z.json), SHA-256 `4a900d18e59b3652f044e8832ff0387201a4021010222cd00e42b656f501b779`, showed no GPU compute process after the three captures. A further stock refresh requested managed memory, prefetch and CUDA Graphs together; the per-device report fields show numeric kernels, managed allocation/prefetch, two graph replays and cleanup passing, but the overall runner remains incomplete for absent SM70 and sets `cuda_execution_validated=false`. [Refresh report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-stock-managed-graph-refresh-v1-20261101T0243Z.json), SHA-256 `435d37a94a0a7d74526453ba6c0c45fdc6157f28f6eb013987ba7fc88b803911`. The final [02:42Z inventory](../evidence/pc3-user-gpu-inventory-v1-20261101T0242Z.json), SHA-256 `7c2ace3fa3461470867d50f784f8e2eb546e1df8b5aab36b82ab0df149cd7630`, again shows no GPU compute process, stock R595.99.02 on the RTX 5080, and the V100 VM active. The V100 remains unavailable to the host process: its PCI function is bound to `vfio-pci`, and guest SSH authentication is denied despite a verified forwarded endpoint and matching pinned host-key fingerprint. No guest command ran; the guest driver and CUDA state remain unknown.

The static `GT200_DEBUGGER` layout audit has two focused tests; the unprivileged PC3 inventory tool has four; the identity-file and collector contracts pass **40/40**; and the RTX selected-device runner passes **70/70**. The complete pinned CPU/software suite passes **2,356/2,356**, zero failures, in **146.77 s**, exit 0 ([report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261101T0223Z.json), SHA-256 `2585439ec9f4d83cceaae47b57961deb25dca4d74061cb4b9cf56bffb9983f59`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261101T0223Z.log`), SHA-256 `3acb836ae2289cdd698fc3a1ec59d8e69e0a68824d8a9acfc57363d2393b8000`). This full-suite result is software-only: it does not qualify the candidate hybrid modules, the V100 guest, mixed-GPU CUDA, UVM, P2P, performance, or endurance.

## R595 per-CPU mm RSS-counter guard — 2026-11-01

R595.112.07 checks `mm->rss_stat[MM_SHMEMPAGES].counters` before `nv_account_mm_alloc()` allocates bookkeeping and later calls `percpu_counter_add()`. The exact Linux 6.8 build enables `NV_PERCPU_MM_COUNTER`, so the guard is compiled in. For the exceptional null-counter case, the helper returns `NULL`; the existing free callback treats a null bookkeeping pointer as a no-op, avoiding a null dereference while leaving GPU-page release intact and skipping only the optional RSS accounting record.

Patch [`0019-open-vm-null-percpu-rss-counter-guard.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0019-open-vm-null-percpu-rss-counter-guard.patch) applies after the page-pool patch against a pinned `nv-vm.c` preimage. Tests compare `nv_account_mm_alloc()` to pinned R595.112.07, verify guard order, and reject preimage drift. The builder suite passes **19/19**. Exact Linux 6.8 full-source build: [report](../evidence/r595-open-linux68-memory-guards-v3-20261101.json), SHA-256 `a7768362bf930bd9aa9d6f2a7ca56bb1c8c12af4186c6955daaa0a4740c47c35`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/r595-open-linux68-memory-guards-v3-20261101.log`), SHA-256 `8b29c05e9e08b2d82dffba4d025e73307ff9cbb264ee698bdb6fb5fa24a75cd4`. This does not validate an actual buggy-kernel state or GPU runtime; no module was installed or loaded.

## R595 read-only mmap PTE protection ordering — 2026-11-01

R595.112.07 moves the read-only protection setup in `nvidia_mmap_helper()` before BAR, peer-I/O, and system-memory mapping. R595.99.02 applied the same VMA flags after mapping. The correction ensures mapping helpers observe the intended `vm_page_prot` and cleared write flags when they install PTEs. This is consistent with Linux MM API guidance that `pgprot` is supplied to `remap_pfn_range()` and VMA protection may need adjustment before mapping ([Linux MM API](https://docs.kernel.org/core-api/mm-api.html)).

Patch [`0018-open-mmap-readonly-before-pte-install.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0018-open-mmap-readonly-before-pte-install.patch) moves only this block and preserves the already-replayed PM access guard. Exact preimage/postimage hashes are checked with no fuzz or offset. Tests verify ordering before both peer-I/O and system-memory map calls, write-flag clearing, and PM guard retention; the builder test module passes **17/17**. The full R595.99.02 module builds against Linux `6.8.0-147-generic`: [report](../evidence/r595-open-linux68-dmabuf-mmap-v2-20261101.json), SHA-256 `c2e781d6507f27e4f75af4aeb5eb323fc308c2c4d0748d9c373186abfa09c863`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/r595-open-linux68-dmabuf-mmap-v2-20261101.log`), SHA-256 `8e2a4de1272f95ef3ecfd2a527eb500f745950344038531d694f640b2a3cfd12`. No hardware, permission-fault, CUDA, or runtime validation occurred; module remains uninstalled and unloaded.

## R595 dma-buf scatter-gather unmap count correction — 2026-11-01

Linux requires `dma_unmap_sg_attrs()` to receive the original entry count supplied to the map call, not the smaller mapped-segment count returned when the DMA layer merges entries ([DMA API](https://docs.kernel.org/core-api/dma-api.html)). R595.99.02 maps this path with `sgt->orig_nents`, assigns the mapped result to `sgt->nents`, and uses that mapped value for segment iteration, but its unmap used `sgt->nents`. Patch [`0017-open-dmabuf-unmap-original-nents.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0017-open-dmabuf-unmap-original-nents.patch) changes only the unmap argument to `sgt->orig_nents`.

The pinned overlay verifies the exact R595.99.02 preimage/postimage and applies with no fuzz or offset. Regression coverage confirms patch reproducibility against the pinned R595.112.07 source, refusal of a changed preimage, and the mapping/unmapping count relationship. Focused builder tests pass **15/15**. Full-source R595.99.02 builds against Linux `6.8.0-147-generic` headers with pinned GCC 13.3: [report](../evidence/r595-open-linux68-dmabuf-original-nents-v1-20261101.json), SHA-256 `a9c5d484174831f426dc9c53279f88f3d60f8b44843bb3b5f7756d41b8384669`; build log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/r595-open-linux68-dmabuf-original-nents-v1-20261101.log`), SHA-256 `1b4e21f8a7886d2c1f877bf36cd6a20e11cb04847301f3405cf20aeb17dc3d64`. Semantic `Module.symvers` ABI is unchanged. Compilation and CPU tests do not establish live DMA, V100, P2P, CUDA, performance, or reliability behavior; candidate modules remain uninstalled and unloaded.

# Validation and performance evidence

## PC3-only RTX 5080 test update — 2026-11-01T04:24Z

PC3's fresh read-only inventory confirms RTX 5080/SM120, PCI `0000:02:00.0`, R595.99.02, and no remaining compute process after testing. The V100 is assigned to the running QEMU guest on `0000:81:00.0`; see [inventory v6](../evidence/pc3-user-gpu-inventory-v6-20261101T0424Z.json), SHA-256 `81c43ee58f445afc5f96fef6ea4721da0d44bf043b2f1c5f2cdad010d4029c6a`.

The [isolated RTX kernel run](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-kernel-phase-v1-20261101T0412Z.json), SHA-256 `7d1fbd3ee78f3436b8213c47756d1c6ccafa9b1349f4c40143725363cd9f2faa`, reports SM120 numeric result `1200 == 1200`, successful cleanup, and two redacted NV01_DEVICE_0 payload snapshots. Separate [CUDA Graph](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-cuda-graphs-v1-20261101T0413Z.json) and [managed-memory/prefetch](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-managed-prefetch-v1-20261101T0414Z.json) runs report two graph launches and managed/prefetch pass, respectively. Each complete wrapper report remains `rtx5080-report-contract-valid-hybrid-incomplete` with exit 77 because only SM120 was available and required SM70 is absent; none establishes hybrid operation.

The [first RTX benchmark run](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-benchmark-baseline-v1-20261101T0413Z.json), SHA-256 `b48a8cbc72762ebb70abd6f6cdb096ace027513226258e26aaeb83c655b9f3ad`, and [second run](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-benchmark-baseline-v2-20261101T0423Z.json), SHA-256 `a510ec391784cc26d94c84e478b2bf1e2dec282f79e1456ba6ddf29d4d611453`, each include nine launch samples and seven pinned-copy samples per size. The selected-device probe reports both RTX benchmarks passed, while full hybrid acceptance remains incomplete and `performance_benchmark_validated` stays false. The 64 MiB median host-to-device bandwidths were 51.03 and 51.24 GB/s; device-to-host were 23.17 and 23.59 GB/s. Median device launch times were 1.263 and 1.271 μs. Run-to-run differences were about 0.4%, 1.8%, and 0.6%; this is only a short repeatability check, not a before/after comparison or measured gain.

## CUDA VMM cross-generation peer-read discriminator — 2026-10-31

The isolated probe [`hybrid_cuda_vmm_peer_probe.cpp`](../src/hybrid_cuda_vmm_peer_probe.cpp) is compiled, not hardware-validated. Current build v3 binds the source, GCC 15 compiler, CUDA 13.1 header, SM70/SM120 fatbin, and binary in `build manifest` (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/vmm-peer-probe-v3-20261031/build-manifest.json`); executable SHA-256 is `3ebe23609679621035c14f2eec895e4df5df0cbe9bd8e00cddde23e8b9cd92e0`. It builds with `-Werror`, dynamically resolves `libcuda`, and passes GCC `-fanalyzer -Werror`. Its VMM mode is `cuMemCreate` → `cuMemAddressReserve`/`cuMemMap` → per-device `cuMemSetAccess`/`cuMemGetAccess` → destination kernel read, without `cuCtxEnablePeerAccess`. If `cuMemUnmap` fails, the probe records that failure, skips dependent `cuMemRelease` and `cuMemAddressFree` calls, and skips the reverse direction so the same process does not accumulate another mapping. The CPU fake-driver regression verifies these decisions, report JSON, direction results, cleanup errors, and numerical pass/fail behavior. The runner independently pins the executable, manifest, and SM70/SM120 fatbin; it also validates exact PCI IDs/model names, VMM mode, idle target GPUs, report structure, timeout handling, and exclusive mode-0600 evidence, while removing `LD_PRELOAD`, `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, and `LD_AUDIT`. Focused VMM fake-driver and runner-contract tests pass **33/33** (log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/vmm-peer-fake-driver-focused-v3-20261031.log`)); these use CPU memory and injected API responses, not a functioning CUDA implementation.

The full pinned CPU/software suite passes **2,288/2,288** with zero failures/errors/skips, in **141.195 s**, exit 0: [report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261031T224223Z.json), SHA-256 `26d3ea218f510166df68231d7ea151870071554272b1200747d60b084b530401`; mode-0600 log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261031T224223Z.log`), SHA-256 `b515f5044661cc2d9dc364de9636487bdf7460ef4f950c1e145fe41237e3a63c`. Qualification remains `software-suite-only`; runtime/hardware validation is false and no candidate module was installed or loaded. No command initialized a GPU in this increment. The VMM probe must run on PC3 only after read-only inventory confirms the V100 and RTX 5080 are visible on the same Linux kernel. A pass would prove numeric mapped-memory reads for that pair/direction only; it still would not identify PCIe/NVLink DMA, production cross-provider P2P, UVM migration, performance, or endurance.

## Opt-in CUDA peer-transfer probe — 2026-10-31

The v3 research binary pins exact SM70 and SM120 PCI devices, queries directional capability, and enables each advertised direction on the destination context. It performs deterministic synchronous and asynchronous 4 KiB `cuMemcpyPeer` copies, then independently runs `hybrid_peer_read` on the destination GPU to dereference the source-context allocation and validate all returned values. The runner validates the v9 report contract. The peer kernel is a stronger result than cross-context copy alone, but its fake-driver test is CPU-only; only a real device run can establish that SM70 and SM120 can dereference each other's allocations. The Driver API documents directional enablement and the same-major-compute-capability condition for immediate access to all peer allocations; this pair differs in major compute capability, so no cross-generation behavior is inferred from compilation. `direct_dma_attested` stays false because the kernel result does not identify the physical route. The existing hybrid provider bridge continues to reject cross-provider peer-object routing.

The v3 fatbin was built for SM70 and SM120, and `cuobjdump --dump-elf-symbols` finds `hybrid_peer_read` as a global entry point in each image (symbol log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/cuda-peer-memory-fatbin-symbols-v3-20261031.log`)). The host C++ source compiled with `-Werror` to the same SHA-256 as the pinned binary ([receipt](../evidence/cuda-peer-memory-werror-build-v1-20261031.json)). Focused CPU fixture/contract tests pass **163/163**, including one-way and no-access paths, direct peer-kernel numeric reads, injected kernel/copy failures, cleanup, compilation of a fake driver with peer APIs omitted, and rejection of incomplete reports: log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/cuda-peer-memory-focused-tests-v3-final2-20261031.log`). At that earlier v3 checkpoint the full suite passed **2,255/2,255** in **142.471 s**: [report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261031T214157Z.json), log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261031T214157Z.log`), and [probe evidence](../evidence/cuda-peer-memory-probe-v3-20261031.json). The latest 2,274-test run, including the VMM runner contracts, is recorded above. Qualification remains `software-suite-only`; these tests do not emulate a GPU.

PC3 remains unreachable (`tailscale status` reports offline; one bounded ping timed out; SSH timed out before executing a remote command). The fresh attempt is [access probe v8](../evidence/pc3-access-probe-v8-20261031.json). No hardware peer-transfer test ran, its GPU inventory and candidate-module state remain unobserved, and no candidate module was installed or loaded. The hardware experiment is prepared in [`README.md`](../README.md); run it on one Linux system that actually sees both PCI devices. The same-process gate remains unmet by separate VM and host results.

## R595 v15 Volta MMU adapter profile and current candidate callback vectors (2026-10-30)

The first strict source audit of the R595 v15 candidate rejected `make_pte_volta` because it was not token-identical to R580. Inspection showed the candidate adds only a GV100-defined `SYS_NON_COHERENT` aperture selector and includes that aperture in the system-address branch. The audit now canonicalizes exactly those two changes and requires the entire normalized remainder to equal the pinned R580 function. It also requires the candidate `hwref/volta/gv100/dev_mmu.h` to match the pinned R580 header byte-for-byte. Any additional source change fails closed. The current exact v15 candidate passes the static audit: [report v2](../evidence/volta-mmu-adapter-audit-r595-v15-v2-20261030T0528Z.json), SHA-256 `9db0dc96dd0dfab2d3dcd9f4269b8e4b5e17b017199ad4aa048df3ad8e4f755c`.

A host harness compiled the selected callback bodies from the exact R580 and R595 v15 trees with `cc -std=gnu11 -O2 -Wall -Wextra -Werror`; all **32** PTE/PDE numerical vectors and **2** expected assertion guards passed. The checked cases include system-coherent/noncoherent, VID, peer mappings including the 47-bit address vector, PDE layout/ATS cases, and poisoned PTEs. [Report](../evidence/volta-mmu-host-harness-r595-v15-v1-20261030T0524Z.json), SHA-256 `6727be65ec104eb28d84925b9e85827b607ee84c60cf47996cf30f09f1135ca4`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/volta-mmu-host-harness-r595-v15-v1-20261030T0524Z.log`), SHA-256 `bd0929135503447a49a6ee2b9fb7fba6801d909927865e427eeb12bc383503cc`.

The harness was challenged with two source mutants. Mapping SYS_NON_COHERENT to the coherent aperture failed the expected BAR1 vector; shifting the peer high address bits failed the 47-bit peer vector. Both were rejected, as recorded in [mutation receipt](../evidence/volta-mmu-host-harness-r595-v15-mutation-check-v1-20261030T0525Z.json), SHA-256 `ddd6cef10ff1f05c3639a592531ab1fca49e2f32cf21d66087812cb36c9fd4f2`.

The full pinned suite then passed **2,229 tests**, process exit code **0**, in **143.951 s**: [report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261030T052604Z.json), SHA-256 `b8c0d8fd932ac63243d77299fb643d5a9a9049fa3eefcd22e9e50369e0eb3901`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261030T052604Z.log`), SHA-256 `7b412221ba329d8372385fad220fabdd92852ad26a2e58160009f89e8977ffc9`.

The old audit's strict-source rejection was not evidence of a CUDA/runtime defect, and the corrected static/profile plus CPU vectors do not establish GPU PTE behavior. ## Canonical reproducibility and isolated V100 P2P fault-injection contract (2026-10-30)

The local runner adds four mutually exclusive `--p2p-fault` modes and a required hash-pinned `--p2p-fault-shim`. The shim matches the exact R580.196.07 RM ioctl encodings at compile time and checks that the descriptor's character-device identity matches `/dev/nvidiactl`; unavailable identity information forwards the real ioctl unchanged. A mode selects an operation family and denies every exact matching four-byte BAR1 object allocation or control request on a previously successful tracked object with the exact pinned payload size. Allocation/control/free transactions serialize tracking changes. Safe process-local reads use `process_vm_readv`; unreadable structures are forwarded. If the 64-entry tracking table fills, the shim emits a dedicated overflow diagnostic and runner validation rejects the capture. The public `ioctl` symbol is implemented by an x86-64 trampoline: only the three pinned RM request numbers enter fixed-signature C code, while every unrelated request tail-jumps to the next provider with all incoming integer argument registers preserved. On the cold path, the trampoline saves RAX/AL and RDI/RSI/RDX/RCX/R8/R9 across symbol resolution, restores them, and tail-jumps directly; no C variadic relay retypes the unknown third argument. Separate fresh fixture processes put no-argument `FIOCLEX` and scalar `_IOW(..., 7)` first. The fixture also checks matching/mismatching descriptor identity, overflow handling, denial, and unrelated requests without opening a GPU device.

The isolated child environment is rebuilt from a minimal `PATH`, locale, and selected GPU UUID; `LD_PRELOAD` and `HYBRID_RM_P2P_FAULT` are added only to the CUDA probe child. Runner validation accepts one or more denial records only when every record matches the selected operation family; the recorded count must agree with the stderr diagnostics. Any overflow or other injector diagnostic invalidates the capture, even when an exact denial also occurred. A nonzero probe exit is accepted as the injected-fault observation and labeled `p2p-fault-injection-report-contract-valid`; it never becomes a successful CUDA run. Fault mode is rejected when combined with phase, benchmark, capture, or `libcuda` override options. The runner contract also checks the pinned stock probe bundle before any credential or remote access.

The first run from the canonical tree failed before compilation because the builder pointed only at a 438 MiB SDK tree in the local mirror. This failure is preserved in canonical logs v1 and v2 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `control/runs/rm-p2p-fault-canonical-focused-v1-20261030.log`). The builder now checks each in-repository candidate for all include directories and all six exact R580.196.07 header hashes, refuses to build if none match, and records the selected source-root path in build-report schema v3. The canonical tree selected `build/experiments/r58019607-proprietary-module-build-v4-20261029/source`; no SDK tree was copied.

The canonical builder produced the same 16,432-byte ELF as v7 (SHA-256 `5b36f90c12f56d3ca1956b8aac61db3d8df9306a96deb1cfd4a81c52683e298e`) with warnings-as-errors, linker hardening, and unchanged pinned C/assembly inputs. See the [canonical v8 build receipt](../evidence/rm-p2p-fault-injector-build-v8-canonical-20261030.json), which records the selected source root, six pinned headers, and 21 non-system dependency hashes. Selector regressions cover missing-primary fallback and fail-closed hash mismatch. A separate fixture interposes `dlsym`, forces `dlsym(RTLD_NEXT, "ioctl")` to return null, and verifies that the cold assembly trampoline returns `-1` with `errno=ENOSYS`; no real ioctl provider is invoked on that path. The fixture bootstrap now uses the x86-64 GLIBC_2.2.5 baseline instead of GLIBC_2.34. From the canonical tree, the UBSan-enabled injector/runner suite passes **13/13** after this change, and the complete `tests.test_run_pc3_v100_guest_reference` contract suite passes **110/110**. ## Exact-target `UvmGpuInfo` layout and value-domain regression (2026-10-29)

A host C probe compiles against the exact R580.196.07 and R595.99.02 UVM headers and executes under UBSan. The probe compares all 35 field offsets and structure size; they match. `NV2080_CTRL_MC_ARCH_INFO_ARCHITECTURE_GV100` is `0x140` in both trees, and all `UVM_VIRT_MODE` values are `0..4` in both. `UVM_LINK_TYPE` differs: R580 uses `NONE=0`, `PCIE=1`, `NVLINK_1..5=2..6`, `C2C=7`; R595 inserts `PCIE_BAR1=2` and shifts `NVLINK_1..5`/`C2C` to `3..8`. The reproducible [value-domain receipt v2](../evidence/r595-hybrid-gpu-info-value-domains-v2-20261029.json) pins each header and fixture hash (receipt SHA-256 `04cbae9eff9582c21a4467c9c844e34c82e473f198619db4fb998add3de3c289`).

The exact production `hybrid580_legacy_gpu_get_info()` callback test verifies all eight mappings, unknown-value rejection, provider-error propagation, full output zeroing on both error paths, and preservation of the other architecture/class/virtualization fields. The focused ABI test file passes **4/4** with UBSan. The full software suite passes **2,179/2,179** in **139.773 s**, exit 0, `software-suite-only`: [report](../evidence/software-verification-r595-hybrid-gpu-info-enum-v6-20261029.json), SHA-256 `050ab3959a1a991fddf6c70f983f4d786f2b7b48aa4d891fafd84470d8bfed1c`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-r595-hybrid-gpu-info-enum-v6-20261029.log`), SHA-256 `cc1bf45b9e7fdfc31918551b2df695cc64c9a99b0234d2270ab384f55c4f9c55`.

This is exact-header compile/execution plus CPU regression evidence. The existing Linux 6.8 candidate module was built and statically audited in the prior increment; this test-only increment did not rebuild, install, or load it. PC3 remains offline, and no GPU/CUDA/UVM runtime, hybrid same-process, P2P, performance, or endurance behavior is validated.

## GV100 selector rows and UVM field-consumer census (2026-10-29)

The R580.196.07 headers define `GV100` architecture `0x140`, implementation `0`, Volta host class `0xC36F`, and Volta CE class `0xC3B5`; the corresponding identifiers appear in the matching R580 UVM host/CE/architecture HAL rows. The R595.99.02 upstream UVM source contains no Volta host/CE row, while the built hybrid UVM receipt records the five staged R580 Volta objects. This is source-level alignment, not proof that the closed provider returns the expected class values or that those rows initialize on a V100. [Selector/dispatch receipt](../evidence/r595-hybrid-gv100-gpu-info-dispatch-v1-20261029.json), SHA-256 `5312999d28b1dac69833e7f2c7a1f5ed0faaafb7cffbdde0bce6277222cc0083`.

A regex-based consumer census over all C/header files in the two pinned UVM trees finds `computeClass` has no direct UVM reads, `virtMode` has two per branch, and `sysmemLink` one per branch. The field counts for `gpuArch` are 18 in R580 and 16 in R595, reflecting branch-specific architecture code; this is not a semantic equivalence result. The [census receipt](../evidence/r595-hybrid-uvm-gpu-info-consumer-census-v1-20261029.json), SHA-256 `5c1b51229297ab2486a674855f1507de8d6d892c39b50f2a3e487faa01e9e3a5`, records the source-tree manifests and limits of the pattern scan.

## R580/R595 UVM `libcuda` payload comparison and CPU suite (2026-10-29)

The updated exact-header comparator compiles the R580 proprietary, R580 Open-candidate, and R595 UVM declarations with GCC 15.2.0, x86-64, and `-std=gnu11 -Wall -Wextra -Werror`. The complete pinned CPU/software suite passes **2,172/2,172** in **127.772 s**, exit 0. Suite report v2 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/software-verification-rea-v100-route-abi-v2-20261029.json`), SHA-256 `fffe3b358df966878785effd704994b7064f1c50accd829d02c0de77e14066da`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-rea-v100-route-abi-v2-20261029.log`), SHA-256 `63bb3370e0da91fae9ac3590ae57080c9ae1b8c66b17e673869f488043ed8aab`. ## Clean full-source R595 build and relocated-path ABI audit (2026-10-29)

The pinned R595.99.02 replay (43 patches), plus exact PFN and softdep overlays, builds successfully for Linux `6.8.0-147-generic` with GCC 13.3. Kbuild exits 0 in **35.710 s** and records **9 warnings**. The output is a 34,189,240-byte `hybrid580_open.ko`, SHA-256 `c7281c742a4800c57d4ba5cde8a333a29577ebb75c237fa539f0ddd5cb703dcb`; `modinfo` verifies name `hybrid580_open`, version `595.99.02`, target `vermagic`, `depends=ecc`, and `pre: ecdh_generic ecdsa_generic`. The clean build's module SHA is not equal to the incremental artifact SHA; the builder preserves that fact rather than calling the outputs identical.

Kbuild embeds its absolute output path in the raw `Module.symvers` module column, so the raw SHA changes across work roots. A normalized digest that changes only that column to the module owner and sorts by symbol matches exactly (`285db44e3d654091312049a2bb94d3b312ed806ec0169ec830a7362d6c56a549`); CRC, symbol, owner and export metadata are all included. The four-module audit then checks actual ELF exports/imports and proves the frontend's recorded Module.symvers input has the same complete normalized Open provider ABI. It passes with zero issues (**301 frontend imports, 328 UVM imports, 81 standard UVM interfaces**). A private Linux 6.8 depmod stage also passes and plans `ecc`, both SPDM prerequisites, Open, legacy, frontend and UVM. Reports: [full build](../evidence/r595-open-linux68-full-replay-v3-20261029.json), [bundle audit v3](../evidence/r595-hybrid-bundle-static-audit-linux68-v3-20261029.json), [depmod plan v4](../evidence/r595-hybrid-depmod-plan-linux68-v4-20261029.json).

The new path-normalization and full-build checks pass **30/30** focused tests. The full pinned CPU/software suite passes **2,157/2,157** in **127.864 s**, exit 0, `software-suite-only`; [report](../evidence/software-verification-r595-full-source-open-audit-v1-20261029.json), SHA-256 `46e68681d3eef5984294c2e81a9caa7dfda1c1840293ecc1aa6a0a629c243a7e`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-r595-full-source-open-audit-v1-20261029.log`), SHA-256 `770b2c32bf59a145407b2d11fcc7d8ded7e9af1b8a59666fce29a7c1f9058b81`.

These are replay, compilation, static ELF/CRC closure and private dependency-planning results. The new full-build module is in `/tmp`; the experiment did not install or load it, did not access a GPU, and does not prove module insertion, RM initialization, CUDA contexts/kernels, UVM migration, same-process V100 + Blackwell execution, P2P, performance, rollback or prolonged stability. The full pinned software suite passes **2,136/2,136** in **129.649 s**, exit 0, software-suite-only; [report](../evidence/software-verification-r595-frontend-crc-contract-fix-v1-20261029T1238Z.json), SHA-256 `65cbeae0bb9c8a281d7d98be4fd756bb87dcf1740c5ee6fbb569408d1ef19267`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-r595-frontend-crc-contract-fix-v1-20261029T1238Z.log`), SHA-256 `2002cc5c62fae1b4f39522dd92bf491d56c10cf88088b86326d7ad9c77f0129e`. Qualification is exact-target compiled/static ABI only. Nothing was installed or loaded; no GPU, RM, CUDA context/kernel, UVM migration, P2P, cross-GPU same-process path, performance, recovery or prolonged stability test occurred.

| PC3 recovery inventory and canary gate | Snapshot v15 confirms both `nvidia-driver-595-open` and its version-exact Xorg dependency are installed at `595.99.02-0ubuntu0.26.04.1`. A direct `dpkg-query`/`dpkg --audit` check and no-download APT simulation reproduce the package state; no mutations occurred. The collector now includes `xserver-xorg-video-nvidia-*`; the earlier apparent missing dependency was a collection-scope omission.
