## Provider-routed Volta CSL contexts (2026-10-21)

The frontend exports all ten R580 `nvUvmInterface*CSL` names and selects the backend from the tracked owner of the UVM device/channel. CSL setup registers the opaque context against both its device and channel parents; it never reads or modifies RM-owned fields in `UvmCslContext`. FaultInfo and its embedded CSL identity are reserved and retired together. Key rotation pins every context first, refuses a mixed-provider list before RM dispatch, and releases all pins afterward. Channel destruction is refused and the frontend pinned while a CSL child remains, preserving provider-owned lifetime even if teardown ordering is unexpected.

The `hybrid-uvm-csl-v5` build report records a2,711,616-byte module for Linux `7.0.0-38-generic`, SHA-256 `9a21fefd45cb7aa1576bd2175459b8460da6eb27185fd24e201bb0f255e6f2d1`; report SHA-256 `56fe071ccf4d4e3f111fbb37be37196147be0ad93ff30db036717290df45ee1a`, mode0600. All209 expected private backend imports resolve without unresolved or duplicate providers, and objtool emits no naked-return warnings. The remaining pahole-version warning is retained in the report. The [static closure audit](../evidence/hybrid-uvm-csl-symbol-coverage-v3-20261021.json) finds all76 standard names and no unresolved UVM imports. The full suite log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-uvm-csl-v3-20261021.log`), SHA-256 `ec5b8b0eafd18b80f8f6dc1930fe3ba8c6c6cb629a10532eab4e0b562ad9a525`, reports580 tests passed in32.936s with exit0.

These results establish compilation, static name/CRC closure, and CPU/UBSan contracts only. The module was not loaded or installed; the backend UVM methods were not invoked against a live RM. No UVM allocation, fault, paging, suspend/resume, same-Linux multi-GPU CUDA, or performance behavior is established. PC3 was not reached: Tailscale ping succeeded, but configured SSH authentication failed and Tailscale SSH rejected an unknown host key under strict checking. No host-key bypass was attempted.

## Targeted `RM_USER_SHARED_DATA` observation (2026-10-21)

The observer's RUSD mode follows only successful `RM_ALLOC` packets for class `0xDE` on `/dev/nvidiactl` and the explicitly selected `/dev/nvidia0`. For each matching packet it snapshots the typed eight-byte `NV00DE_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` at entry and exit, validates the request/outer packet shape, and emits only `classPayloadBytes` and the 64-bit `polledDataMask`; addresses and handles are omitted. The mode is isolated from other capture and memory modes. A complete empty trace qualifies as an absence observation only when the nested-read count is zero.

The independent repeat-build report [`rm-ioctl-observer-rusd-build-v4`](../evidence/rm-ioctl-observer-rusd-build-v4-20261021.json), SHA-256 `d9e6ab2dec22233382ca67436a32ef2ddc3a1b39ff27ffc5a414daec2ec01578`, reports matching class ABI measurements for pinned R580.178.04 and R595.99.02: 8-byte structure, field offset0, and identical clock/perf/memory/power/thermal/PCI/fan constants. Its observer binary is `e4860e93c68c51d35f34c6347463426280fe1a1eda2546894c0ce8eb5a40cddc`; it was uploaded only as a read-only child observer and never installed or loaded.

On RTX5080/R595.99.02, the selected standard device-memory CUDA probe passed its SM120 architecture and five synchronous/asynchronous sizes and cleanup; the combined probe returned77 because SM70 is absent. One successful RUSD class allocation was seen, both snapshots agreed on mask0, and the complete observer trace had no errors. The [mode-0600 report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-rusd-allocation-v3-20261021.json) has SHA-256 `cd1f640c2b9848d27b58829459d5474778fef18334105987b5e010ee0459bb76`.

On the separate V100 guest/R580.178.04, the standard SM70 CUDA probe passed and cleanup passed. Its targeted trace completed without errors but contained no RUSD class allocation and zero nested payload snapshots. The [mode-0600 report](../evidence/pc3-v100-rusd-allocation-v4-20261021.json) has SHA-256 `a6ec7c05d4827f69d2aeb7a78fa62a974928d696caccb0ebd925ed1e3e99f539`. This establishes absence only for the observed default V100 probe, not other workloads. Neither capture exercised the experimental frontend or a co-located hybrid process.


## Typed RUSD allocation route and RTX map/mmap evidence (2026-10-21)

The stock RTX5080/R595.99.02 lifecycle trace resolves one successful RM_USER_SHARED_DATA allocation and one successful MAP_MEMORY request to class 0xDE. The selected backend-file identity captured for the map matches one successful mmap record, with the descriptor role agreeing. This matching is computed inside the report validator; opaque identities are not exposed. The full trace contains 26 MAP_MEMORY requests, 26 mmap calls and 23 successful unmaps. See [the mode-0600 report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-rusd-map-lifecycle-v2-20261021.json), SHA-256 3e7de1db18d95c6fb45393f7c2511e80709871c0e2115daff96571d330989f6c. It is an installed R595 reference only.

The private R580 Open and proprietary backends now expose typed class-0xDE allocation methods. Their report/module identities are recorded in the [Open build report](../evidence/private-open-rusd-v2-20261021.json) and [proprietary build report](../evidence/private-proprietary-rusd-v4-20261021.json). The default frontend build [resolves both providers](../evidence/hybrid-frontend-rusd-default-v3-20261021.json), with all 67 backend imports resolved. The C/UBSan tests execute the extracted backend methods using fake RM callbacks. The privacy-preserving [correlation summarizer](../tools/summarize_rusd_map_mmap_lifecycle.py) reads the pinned report, checks that the RUSD-class map and successful mmap share an opaque backend-file identity and role, and writes counts only; its [summary](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-rusd-map-mmap-correlation-v1-20261021.json) is mode 0600. The full suite passes 508 tests in lifecycle v1 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-rusd-lifecycle-v1-20261021.log`), SHA-256 295fed5f5e8583028872c5a437d84a3c0ac5aaa1c3cee3ef9866c14f2e2cd5c7.

No experimental object has been loaded or installed. The stock trace does not exercise the new frontend, VFS callbacks, or either R580 backend. The separate V100 probe had no RUSD allocation in its default CUDA path, which is workload-specific. Same-Linux CUDA, actual RUSD creation/free on both backends, RUSD mmap through the frontend, UVM, P2P, and performance remain unvalidated.

## RM_CONTROL layout result and limits

The observer captured only fixed ioctl and RM_CONTROL headers, not nested parameter bytes or pointer addresses. All 165 RTX5080 and 170 V100 RM_CONTROL syscalls returned transport success; one RTX command `0x2080012f` carried embedded status `0x56`. The corrected comparison records three actual parameter-size differences and separately records four call-frequency differences. Comparison v1 is retained but superseded because that first implementation mislabeled frequency differences as size drift.

The independently compiled R580/R595 SDK probe confirms sizes `132/68` for `GPU_GET_NAME_STRING`, `1464/1664` for `GPU_QUERY_ECC_STATUS`, and `13344/13864` for `NVLINK_GET_NVLINK_STATUS`. Exact archives, SDK header hashes, compiler version and fixture/tool hashes are in the linked report. R580 source `rmapi/control.c` checks the caller's declared size against the registered method size and returns an error on mismatch; internal GPU method dispatch also checks exact `paramSize`. This establishes that arbitrary cross-release forwarding is unsafe for these methods. It does not show that the captured nested payloads were malformed or justify writing a translator without the command-specific semantics.

The R580.178.04 product appendix lists both the Tesla V100 and RTX 5080, but NVIDIA's same-release module guide says Volta requires the proprietary module flavor and Blackwell requires Open, and the flavors cannot be installed or loaded together. This supports using two same-version backends with one version-matched userspace as the prototype target; it does not prove those backends can be unified or that the private frontend is CUDA-compatible. No experimental module was loaded, and both reference GPUs remain on separate Linux kernels.

Independent broker review identified an unproven assumption: error status plus zero output handle had been treated as absence of allocation. Two regression assertions reproduced that gap; the corrected broker quarantines every backend allocation failure. The full fixture also tests reverse cleanup, retained failed-free ownership, errors in either status channel, missing status, aliased file state, delayed publication, endpoint-array mutation and reentrancy. Actual runtime locking, RM global-state isolation and file teardown remain outside this CPU contract.

The first Kbuild broker attempt failed because `nv.h` selected userspace `stddef.h` before the OS macros were established. Including the vendor `nvtypes.h` first fixes that recorded compilation failure. The corrected objects compile with `NV_KERNEL_INTERFACE_LAYER`, `NVRM` and the actual kernel flags; they are not installed or executed. The separate official Open580 module build has10,857 objtool naked-return/MITIGATION_RETHUNK warnings plus other logged warnings. A zero build exit does not qualify those mitigation paths for loading.

The separate proprietary build retains44,969 naked-return/MITIGATION_RETHUNK and1,758 frame-pointer objtool warnings. No warning was suppressed to make this result pass. Its zero GPL-only import count applies to that proprietary-only module, not to the two-core object. The two wrapper builds validate compilation/linkage under their own source conditions; they do not validate stock coexistence or a shared frontend.

## Typed RM_ALLOC and device-instance boundary

The header-only [allocation comparison](../evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-rm-alloc-header-summary-v1-20261021.json) validates93 RTX5080/R595 and96 V100/R580 fixed NVOS64 allocation headers, with zero syscall failures. Both traces contain a successful `NV01_DEVICE_0` class (`0x80`) under ROOT_CLIENT (`0x41`), a non-null `pAllocParms`, and outer `paramsSize=0`. The nested bytes were never read. Pinned R580 source uses class metadata to size/copy allocation parameters, then `deviceConstruct_IMPL()` reads `NV0080_ALLOC_PARAMETERS.deviceId`; the public `GPU_GET_DEVICE_IDS` command returns a device-instance bitmask. Therefore a zero outer `paramsSize` does not establish that the class has no parameters, and this object cannot be safely forwarded without a typed copy and per-backend device-instance map.

The exact R580/R595 headers compile to identical typed layouts: `NV0080_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` is56 bytes with `deviceId` at offset0, and `GPU_GET_DEVICE_IDS` carries one4-byte mask. [Probe report v3](../evidence/rm-control-layout-r580-r595-v3-20261021.json) records source/header/fixture hashes. The new [`hybrid580-device-instance-map.c`](../src/hybrid580-device-instance-map.c) validates that per-GPU ID-info records exactly cover both backend masks, then assigns deterministic frontend device-instance bits while preserving same-backend groups and separating equal local IDs from different backends. Five CPU/UBSan scenarios pass. The map object is present in [frontend Kbuild v1](../evidence/frontend-device-instance-map-v1-20261021.json), which links with30 exact backend imports, zero unresolved/duplicate providers, and active objtool. This is build evidence only: the helper is not called by the frontend, mask query is not exposed, no module was loaded, and no runtime mask, nested allocation field, or child handle was observed. The next implementation must add the typed backend mask query and wire this map to `NV01_DEVICE_0` allocation plus child-handle ownership.

## What the real probe covers

The probe creates every targeted context first, verifies context/device identity, then runs architecture-specific and vector-add kernels for sizes1,31,256,4096,65537. Both synchronous copies and pinned-host asynchronous stream operations check every output. Optional managed allocation, CPU/GPU prefetch, and two CUDA Graph replays have now passed separately on the stock V100/R580 guest and RTX5080/R595 host using the current-source build. It holds contexts resident until device testing finishes and includes cleanup failures in final qualification. The two-device success path is covered against a fake CPU driver; it has not run on the actual required pair. Kernel execution between devices is sequential. Cross-device UVM migration, concurrent kernels, P2P, reset recovery, and long-duration behavior still require dedicated tests.

The runner records resolved host/library/fatbin identities, raw output, exit code, timestamps and validation errors. By default missing hardware returns77. Invalid or contradictory reports fail. The software verification record separately stores its test log and input hashes; an OpenRM test skipped for missing pinned sources is not equivalent to running it.

PC3 RTX5080 reference: `evidence/pc3-rtx5080-selected-cuda-20261019.json`, UUID and PCI0000:02:00.0, Driver API13020. Only the RTX5080 context was created. The C++ expected-PCI guard checks unique inventory before contexts, in both inventory and real-kernel processes; CPU tests also exercise an extra GPU and a wrong single GPU. The remote runner captures exact environment selection and SHA identities. It does not independently attest driver-internal enumeration/autoload behavior.

PC3 V100 reference: [hardware evidence](../evidence/pc3-v100-sm70-reference-v4-20261020.json), reached inside VM `pc3-v100` through a strict, pinned SSH path. The guest reports one Tesla V100-SXM2-16GB at `0000:00:05.0`, driver580.178.04, kernel6.8.0-147-generic; the host function remains at `0000:81:00.0` under `vfio-pci`. The probe's SM70 image passed architecture, sync and async vector checks at1,31,256,4096,65537 with cleanup passing and0MiB used both before and after. This validates the installed V100 guest stack only. The runner verifies that the sole host fingerprint accepted for the exact forwarded endpoint is the pinned key and rejects untrusted known-hosts ownership/mode; a generated unrelated-host fingerprint is covered by a negative regression test. No Blackwell context, mixed-generation process, concurrency, UVM migration, graph, P2P or performance claim follows.

The separate parent V100/R580 harness (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `../hardware/v100-r580/README.md`) completed its CUDA-X12.9, RDC/device-link and library smoke gates on 2026-10-20 after physical cooling confirmation. See the [acceptance report](../evidence/pc3-v100-r580-acceptance-final-20261020.json), [metadata correction audit](../evidence/pc3-v100-r580-acceptance-report-correction-20261020.json) and raw run archive (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/pc3-v100-r580-acceptance-final-20261020.tar.gz`). The earlier short vector probe remains a separate, narrower run.

The instrumented rerun retains599 IOCTL metadata lines and the same successful numerical contract. All100 observed RM_ALLOC calls use48-byte packets. The summary reports no payload class/pointer/flag/status observation and no dedicated UVM-workload validation. The broker's new NVOS64 entry sends genuine48-byte SDK packets, rejects non-null rights/parameters and nonzero flags, and shares rollback/lifetime semantics with NVOS21. It is CPU-tested and compiled only.

The installed-driver guest run passed all21 recorded steps with exit0 on Tesla V100-SXM2-16GB, UUID `GPU-42d06a85-027a-75ea-7857-a8de27ad465e`, guest PCI `0000:00:05.0`, kernel `6.8.0-147-generic`, R580.178.04, CUDA Driver API13000. The exact bundle manifest had6,347 entries and SHA-256 `1d075b689d4f74144a7a648cb3be95fc551104e8cd1efbe69a70d8e17d1838a1`.

The run covers integrity/runtime checks, SM70 and PTX8.8 compilation and execution, negative architecture controls, RDC/device-link, selecting SM70 from a mixed SM70+SM120 fatbin, and deterministic CUDA-X12.9 cuBLAS, cuFFT, cuRAND, cuSOLVER and cuSPARSE tests. Cooling was physically confirmed before execution. The before/after samples were51°C and52°C against NVML GPU_MAX83°C (32°C and31°C margin); the 5°C minimum guard remained satisfied while `temperature.gpu.tlimit` reported `N/A`.

The archive is hash-pinned in the report. No experimental module was installed or loaded. This establishes the V100-only guest stack; same-Linux co-location with Blackwell, UVM migration, P2P, concurrency, prolonged stability and performance remain unvalidated.

## Performance report

**No hybrid-versus-stock GPU comparison or performance gain has been measured. No percentage gain, token throughput, TTFT or inference-latency improvement is claimed.** A one-run stock RTX 5080 microbenchmark is documented above as diagnostic evidence only; the multi-run stock references remain in [`PERFORMANCE_REFERENCE.md`](PERFORMANCE_REFERENCE.md). Build duration is not GPU performance, and synthetic Driver API tests cannot measure launch latency or bandwidth.

Before benchmarking a functional candidate, refresh paired stock controls for R580 on V100 and the working Open branch on Blackwell with the same probe, toolchain, host/PCI topology, user libraries, inputs, clocks/power policy, temperature range and warmup. Record compiler/SM image selection, model hash, precision/quantization, prompt/output lengths, batching and concurrency. Do not attribute toolkit, library or workload changes to the driver.

Measure launch distributions with CPU overhead separated from device work, transfer latency/bandwidth by actual path (pinned/pageable, sync/async, P2P capability queried), memory footprint and CPU utilization. Verify outputs throughout. For inference report throughput, TTFT and per-token latency distributions with repetitions and raw samples. If P2P is unavailable, report the staged path rather than implying direct transfer. Extend duration and error-recovery tests only after basic numerical, memory and context tests pass.

Modernization candidates remain hypotheses: newer UVM allocation/migration/VA invalidation paths and reduced launch overhead require a correct Volta HAL first. Removed capability fields are semantic changes, not demonstrated improvements. No software change can add Blackwell FP4 Tensor Cores or exclusive instructions to GV100.

## Private resource increment

Earlier resource-only software run: **80 tests passed, zero skipped**, retained in [verification v6](../evidence/software-verification-v6-20261019.json).

Patches0004–0007 are checked against the pinned R580 source with executable CPU contracts. Allocation and both cdev-add failures release only acquired resources; two dynamically assigned majors are exercised. The complete actual `nv_get_file_private`/`nv_put_file_private` functions reject foreign majors/operations, validate control and regular minors, and balance reference lifetimes. Kernel APIs are substituted, not executed in Linux. The unmodified vendor signed/unsigned MINOR comparison is compiled with the matching warning disabled in that host fixture; this does not suppress the module's objtool warnings.

The original20-byte procfs buffer is shown to truncate the private name; the patched buffer produces the exact22-byte path including NUL. All4324 capability table paths are unique and match the real sscanf readers, including GPU31/GI14/CI7. Lifecycle fragments execute success/failure paths with forbidden NVSwitch/platform calls undeclared, while ELF metadata checks the absence of public autoload aliases. NVLink core remains. These establish bounded source contracts, not actual registration, discovery by libcuda, isolation from a live NVIDIA driver or V100 functionality.

## Projection and return-thunk increment

The source-extracted projection contract executes38 scenarios within two unittest methods: malformed/duplicate provider, immutable copy, temporary module pin, foreign/alias/error backing files, ownership and control/GPU state checks, balanced references and writer withdrawal waiting for an active callback. The pthread model does not prove Linux IRQ/lockdep behavior, module unload, close races or RM operation. Its intentionally held test callback is a synchronization fixture, not an allowed production callback.

The real Kbuild/ELF result has95 exact exports,432 resolved kernel imports and104 GPL-only imports unchanged in count. Four added reader/writer lock imports are ordinary exports. The return-thunk RM is freshly built from unmodified source with the exact compiler flag. The integrated module has zero objtool/naked-return warnings with objtool still enabled;30 other warnings remain. The proprietary backend and full kernel mitigation behavior are not qualified by that result.

Earlier full software run: **89 tests passed, zero skipped**, with artifact checks in [verification v7](../evidence/software-verification-v7-20261019.json). No hardware, VM or remote operation was performed for this increment. No throughput, latency or GPU performance improvement is claimed.

The broker runs82 scenarios:33 raw and49 opaque, across both NVOS packet forms. No Linux LSM, file-table/module-unload race, deferred fput or hardware behavior is inferred. The initial control-session full build failed on duplicate rs_access types: wrapper and SDK use separate pragma-once files. A compiled negative reproduces that conflict. The builder overlays an unchanged SDK nvos.h beside the wrapper's declarations; a positive compile and fresh v2 module build pass. Source SDK bytes and notices are preserved. The failed report `private-open-control-session-20261019.json` remains negative evidence.

Latest software/artifact verification: **95 tests passed, zero skipped**, [v8](../evidence/software-verification-v8-20261019.json). No hardware, remote or VM test was run in this increment; no performance gain is claimed.

## Typed observer and proprietary backend increment

Eight real ptrace CPU tests validate entry/exit SDK field decoding on /dev/null (ENOTTY), threads/signals, bad pointers/sizes, x32, invalid FDs, owned-child timeout/interruption cleanup and new output requirements. These are OS-observer tests, not RM/GPU tests. Optional `--ioctl-observer` in the acceptance/remote runners requires the PCI guard, archives observer hashes and raw JSON, and rejects incomplete/error/wrong-scope observations. Remote preflight preserves exact UUID/PCI selection and installed-driver binding before the traced process starts.

The kernels still pass architecture, all five sync/async sizes and cleanup, with hybrid false/missing70/exit77. Two real NVOS64 ROOT requests fit the broker input slice; output alias normalization and multi-root/frontend FREE-parent behavior are now informed by real observations. This run does not use an experimental driver and does not touch V100/VM. No timing or performance claim is drawn from ptrace.

The [private proprietary build](../evidence/private-proprietary-notices-v3-20261019.json) preserves the core/license, compiles100 exports and303 imports with zero GPL-only, and passes54 session/38 projection/19 PCI model scenarios. ## Initial ROOT-only frontend (historical)

[Frontend build v4](../evidence/frontend-control-v4-20261019.json) links a separate module against both real private backends.32 imports resolve uniquely, ten through ordinary backend exports; metadata names the two dependencies, and objtool remains enabled without warnings.32 CPU scenarios execute actual frontend source plus the real broker under OS/RM doubles, including two clients, out-of-order free, ROOT-parent alias, copy faults, no-ID quarantine, capacity, concurrent allocation/close and contradictory ID reuse before rollback. This is not execution of Linux VFS or either RM binary. One compiler-tooling pahole version warning remains in the log.

Current integrated suite: **119 tests passed, zero skipped**, log `evidence/logs/software-verification-v9-20261019.log`. Broker scenarios now86 after four ROOT alias regression cases; these are counted inside suites rather than additional unittest methods. This status predates the forthcoming canary userspace harness and does not qualify it.

All20 backend imports resolve exactly once; no unresolved or duplicate imports are present. Objtool is active with no objtool warnings; one pahole version warning is retained. The report records `loaded_or_installed=false`, `device_registered=false`, `real_rm_executed=false`, `gpu_accessed=false` and `cuda_compatibility_established=false`.

The frontend source/CPU suite covers50 C scenarios under UBSan, including BDF ownership on both backends, catalog queries built from locked inventory snapshots, empty/error/duplicate cases, each large-buffer allocation failure, strict request validation, modeled fd-install ordering, `O_CLOEXEC`, copyout rollback, backend-specific ioctl/projection and the control-parent/session lifetime. Both successful route cases include a fake `NV_ESC_REGISTER_FD` request and require the control/GPU projections to resolve to the same backend. Catalog tests verify deterministic synthetic IDs, backend-local collisions across backends, unique nonzero IDs within one backend, ordered attach-list partitioning, repeated-ID preservation up to496 occurrences on one backend, invalid-ID rejection and failure atomicity under UBSan. The catalog helper retains coverage through496 inventory entries and the prior Kbuild object check against Linux7.0.0-38 headers: [report](../evidence/gpu-catalog-kbuild-v1-20261021.json), object SHA-256 `a2dd88d733fa6e4666f14e201b47b88b9a42c9335a9d660afe73ea255dd15948`. Fresh full verification passed **242 tests, zero skipped**: `software-verification-v43` (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-v43-attach-plan-20261021.log`), SHA-256 `9a6c35c8725ea9b76a619520d6cb465a41c8e96e0811e3df8999d3d6bac91a68`. All execution above is source/build/CPU-model evidence only. No module was loaded, no `/dev/hybrid580ctl` was registered, and no NVIDIA RM ioctl, CUDA call or GPU ran through the new frontend.

The pure attach planner mirrors the pinned R580 loop's repeated-ID behavior, but zero placeholders are accepted/skipped by the RM while the private planner currently rejects zero as an invalid frontend ID. This historical paragraph predates wiring the attach planner into the frontend. The captured selected GPU-file map/`.mmap` route and a narrow `.poll` route are now modeled and compiled, but neither has run through the Linux VFS or a real frontend session. Remaining requirements include standard NVIDIA device enumeration and GPU-ID translation, end-to-end `NV_ESC_REGISTER_FD`, child objects and general RM controls, frontend mmap/page-fault and event waitqueue validation, RM event-object management, UVM, P2P and same-process numerical kernels. The RTX5080 and V100 installed-driver references run on separate Linux kernels and do not raise this frontend's evidence level.

## Selected GPU-file map and mmap capture (2026-10-21)

The guarded V100 runner gained an isolated `--map-memory` observer mode. It stages the pinned R580 observer, runs the existing device-memory SM70 probe, and validates schema v13, the exact redacted NVOS33/NVOS34 packet shapes, selected-fd open/dup/close invariants, and successful map-to-mmap correlation on the same opaque GPU file identity. It rejects combinations with the broader ioctl/strace or managed-memory captures. Unit tests include malformed request IDs, altered VMA file identities, failed mmap, truncated capture and scope widening.

The current [R580 V100 report](../evidence/pc3-v100-map-mmap-v2-20261021.json) is mode0600 and SHA-256 `2d9ea001d5e9866815af9e1a5a08c45bf95576ac5f1514340929e57a4ede6fae`. It records the existing guest (`pc3-v100`, kernel6.8.0-147, driver580.178.04, V100 at guest BDF `0000:00:05.0`), probe exit0 and `control-passed`, Driver API13000, SM70 numerical checks and cleanup. The report also pins the local runner source (`a4b11606b0c3652e0d4b60f79c8a8ee5017b91c630b28e6f973fef87b86cc9dc`). The observer trace is complete with no errors and complete fd identity capture:37 opens,7 dups,25 NVOS33 map packets of56 bytes,25 `mmap` calls,23 NVOS34 unmaps of32 bytes, and40 closes. All25 map calls and23 unmaps have successful transport/RM status; the strict correlator finds three successful map→mmap pairs on the same GPU file identity. It reads only the fixed packet fields listed by schema v13; raw addresses, path strings, handle values and offset values are omitted. V1 is preserved as the prior capture.

Comparison with the [RTX5080/R595 report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-map-mmap-v5-20261021.json) shows the same observed ioctl IDs and fixed sizes on these two runs (`NVOS33` request `0xc038464e`,56 bytes; `NVOS34` request `0xc020464f`,32 bytes), and three correlated pairs on each branch. The RTX run has26 map/mmap calls and exits77 because the combined probe also requires the absent SM70 image; its SM120 reference passes. Allocation lengths and flags differ between the workloads. This supports the selected-file mapping design for the observed paths only; it is not a universal cross-branch ABI guarantee.

The experimental frontend's own `.mmap` route is represented in the C/UBSan model and compiles against Linux7.0.0-38 with `vma_set_file` imported from `vmlinux`; [build report](../evidence/frontend-gpu-mmap-v2-20261021.json) has module SHA-256 `bc8414df1e8392effd135684238b81963981577a1e04712496b7aa7dd2fc884d`. No experimental module was loaded. Neither stock trace exercises the frontend VFS, its `struct file` replacement, faults, VMA split/fork, deferred close, UVM, or a CUDA allocation through the hybrid code. These gates remain open despite the installed-driver map evidence.

## Selected GPU-file poll delegation (2026-10-21)

The pinned R580 Open and proprietary wrappers and R595 Open source install the same `nvidia_poll` callback for GPU files. It derives event state from the native `struct file`, checks that file's `O_NONBLOCK` flag, and registers the RM-owned waitqueue through `poll_wait`. The frontend's narrow `.poll` route now obtains the BDF-selected session file, mirrors the frontend `O_NONBLOCK` bit with single-copy reads/writes, passes the native `poll_table` through unchanged, and drops its temporary backend reference. No frontend transaction mutex is acquired.

The C/UBSan model covers both backend routes and readiness masks, file/waitqueue identity, no-callback failure, control-file no-readiness, `O_NONBLOCK` enable and disable, select-style backend-file retention, and epoll-style frontend reference retention across descriptor close. Full software verification v71 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-v71-poll-doc-sync-v1-20261021.log`) passed358 tests with zero skips; SHA-256 `6c3bde272f58f2d272fa5fd5f65d860994a55c0f120c3bdb8eedda6a3f5e64b4`.

The [Linux7.0.0-38 build](../evidence/frontend-gpu-poll-v3-20261021.json) compiled an882,872-byte module with SHA-256 `fde6c62f57b28af13f3f6a1d2fcef50bac455f53ca0099ed6dc6f535cbc1137b`; all imports resolve, there are no duplicate providers, objtool is active with zero objtool/naked-return warnings, and the sole build warning is the pahole-version mismatch. The report confirms unchanged inputs, `loaded_or_installed=false` and `real_rm_executed=false`. The model does not execute Linux poll/epoll/VFS, RM event posting, waitqueue wakeup/POLLFREE or teardown; current V100/RTX stock captures do not establish that the numerical vector probe calls poll. This remains source/build/CPU-model evidence, not hardware validation.

## Cross-backend attach transaction (2026-10-21)

Fresh Open R580 build: [`private-open-control-attach-v2`](../evidence/private-open-control-attach-v2-20261021.json), module SHA-256 `4cb01979ce75fd0b16cd913fa211b9adf0f90f2c4abbafaaac7ae8567bd7f576`;107 private exports. It factors the native attach pin/rollback helper and exports `hybrid580_open_control_session_attach_v1`. The proprietary wrapper build [`private-proprietary-control-attach-v5`](../evidence/private-proprietary-control-attach-v5-20261021.json), module SHA-256 `2977d90de42e06f9357bb778f6756ad1e56bd8debd6c1f9911a357f883148913`, has108 exports,303 kernel imports and zero GPL-only. It preserves the original `MODULE_LICENSE("NVIDIA")` and RM object and calls the properly namespaced legacy file helper.

The new frontend [`frontend-control-attach-v1`](../evidence/frontend-control-attach-v1-20261021.json) builds to a 567,424-byte module, SHA-256 `caaf466dc523362549c113b696f186806dc04814c5095d1cd201e953a65b6c49`. Its22 backend imports resolve exactly once; no unresolved or duplicate providers remain. Objtool is active with no objtool warning; the retained pahole version warning is tooling metadata. The builder accepts explicit Open/legacy source trees so this artifact is tied to the new matched backend API headers and Module.symvers.

The frontend accepts both the direct size-encoded native attach command and `NV_ESC_IOCTL_XFER_CMD`. It translates caller-order synthetic IDs to backend-local IDs, preserves duplicate requests and zero placeholders (zero is omitted from backend partitions), creates a clean candidate control-session pair, attaches each nonempty partition, and atomically publishes the pair only if all required backend calls succeed. A failed backend attach leaves the previous pair selected, quarantines further stateful use and releases candidate session references outside locks. Backend `fput` may defer RM cleanup, so this does not prove synchronous rollback.

Coverage includes60 actual C frontend scenarios, of which10 target attach success, direct/XFER forms, second-backend failure, candidate-creation retry, stateful ordering, malformed/copy-fault/repeat/zero cases and a failed GPU open followed by attach. The extracted Open and proprietary control-session CPU harness executes67 scenarios for invalid parameters, PM/stack failures, reference pins, local rollback, zeros/repeats and cleanup. Full software verification passed242 tests, zero skipped, in v45 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-v45-control-attach-20261021.log`), SHA-256 `805a5eb6d329825a3ba7bd244d3e257eebc692f31391d050ac0612d907d6e71e`. Failed v44 is retained as a stale-default-probe invocation; the passing rerun pins both `HYBRID_PROBE` and `HYBRID_CUDA_PROBE` to `build/probe-managed-prefetch-v2/hybrid_cuda_probe`.

All of this remains source/CPU/build evidence: no experimental module was installed or loaded, no VFS/provider ran, and no NVIDIA RM ioctl or CUDA call went through this frontend. Standard `/dev/nvidiaN`, child/general RM controls, UVM, P2P and same-process kernel execution remain outstanding. The later GPU-ID/attached-ID metadata routes also have no real-RM validation. The two installed-driver GPU references run on separate Linux kernels and do not qualify the hybrid route.

## Prepared canary harness

[Canary v3](../evidence/frontend-canary-v3-20261019.json) compiles against authentic SDK packet declarations and kernel expectation. Six tests execute51 syscall-double process scenarios: default preflight without control open, exact metadata/binding guards, character-device checks before/after open, both allocation forms, distinct IDs/canonical class, reverse FREE, status/transport/copy/close failures, malformed inputs and new-report refusal. The production executable and even its actual host preflight have never run.

The initial fixture missed GNU libc's fortified `__readlink_chk` alias. This caused four local binding-symlink checks to return false before any control-device open. The report records the exact paths; those observations do not establish PCI-device absence. The corrected fixture wraps both entry points and checks `nm -u` for unwrapped OS entry points before any execution. A compiled-only negative witness reproduces the missing fortified wrapper; it is not executed. Earlier builder v1 also preserves the negative-witness optimization mismatch; successful v3 pins source/test/tool/compiler/SDK dependencies and all logs. No VM, GPU device or real canary execution occurred in these fixture tests.

Latest integrated verification: **160 tests passed, zero skipped**, fresh log software verification v16 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-v16-20261020.log`), SHA-256 `899e537d450b905f9db5d97e7f3261a2b60f0154226e0df5c2ac966038a910e5`. The dedicated V100 transport/qualification suite also passes19 tests. The Open and proprietary core bytes remain pinned; canary production binary was not run. RTX5080 and V100 installed-driver hardware references are separate from all experimental-module/source/CPU results. The V100 guest reference passed, but same-Linux canary work still requires a verified co-located topology and a separate host-specific recovery/authorization gate.

Local package tests cover deterministic bytes, standalone verification, expected SHA/kernel mismatch, corrupt/missing/extra/duplicate/link/traversal entries, manifest contracts, decompression budgets, gzip trailer/CRC/extra payload, output preservation, producer drift, query environment isolation, real hash/parse ABA substitution and exact original firmware coverage. Failed-before-fix logs are referenced in STATE. Independent review confirmed the ABA correction with two separate substitution reproductions. The verifier reads one compressed stream to EOF and hashes those exact bytes; a digest read from the archive itself is not a trust anchor. No payload extraction, module execution or GPU access is part of these tests.

Fresh PC3 RTX5080 run [raw report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-postcanary-reference-v1-20261020.json) and [typed ROOT summary](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-postcanary-root-summary-v1-20261020.json): installed595.99.02, selected SM120 architecture kernel passes; sync/async lengths1,31,256,4096,65537 pass; cleanup passes. The observer recorded188 calls without trace errors, including two broker-compatible ROOT allocations. Overall probe exits77 with explicit `missing_sm=[70]` because that run selected the RTX5080 only.

Independent package/source review: `evidence/canary-package-independent-review-v1-20261019.json`. Two actual v1 archive builds are byte-identical; an isolated standalone verifier outside the repository checks all94 files without extracting or executing them. v2 exposed an overly strict valid-padding bound and was not published.21 block-position cases now pass after correction, alongside all corruption/trailer/ABA negatives. Current final archive identity is in `evidence/canary-package-reproducibility-v3-20261019.json`; archive integrity and hardware qualification are separate.

## Cross-branch ROOT protocol comparison (2026-10-20)

The read-only observer captured 195 records from the V100 guest under R580.178.04 and 188 from the PC3 host RTX5080 under R595.99.02. Each trace contains two successful NVOS64 ROOT creations with `hRoot=0`, NULL parameters/rights, zero flags, and output class ROOT_CLIENT; each has one confirmed root FREE whose parent equals its root. The reproducible [comparison report](../evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-root-protocol-comparison-v3-20261020.json) normalizes PCI BDFs, checks the source report and summary hashes, reruns the typed summarizer, and records the exact matching input/free shapes. Tool: [compare_rm_root_protocols.py](../tools/compare_rm_root_protocols.py), covered by five comparator tests.

The RTX5080's SM120 kernels and five sync/async sizes passed, but its overall dual-architecture probe correctly exits77 with `missing_sm=[70]`. The original traces contain ordinary CUDA root requests; the bounded follow-up below exercises a nonzero-`hRoot` request on installed R595. No experimental module was loaded, and no hybrid CUDA, UVM, P2P or performance result follows.

The bounded [root-ID probe](../src/rm_root_id_probe.c) consumed a V100 ID selected from that hash-pinned trace and ran on PC3's installed RTX5080/R595.99.02. Both ioctl transport results and embedded statuses were zero; the returned `hObjectNew` equaled `hRoot`, the ROOT FREE succeeded, and the temporary executable was removed. [Report v3](../evidence/pc3-rm-root-id-v100-to-rtx5080-v3-20261020.json) pins the helper, runner, remote script and source-trace hashes and confirms target identity before and after. The first orchestration attempt failed before ioctl because its multiline SSH command was not shell-quoted and is retained as report v1; v2 also passed but predates the final provenance/postflight fields.

The reverse [RTX5080-to-V100 report](../evidence/pc3-rm-root-id-rtx5080-to-v100-v2-20261020.json) also passed as a one-shot test: the V100 guest under R580.178.04 returned the RTX-observed ID unchanged, both statuses passed on allocation and FREE, and its UUID/PCI/driver matched before and after. The unprivileged guest helper and staged PC3 payload were removed. Its first wrapper attempt failed before reaching the guest because `tempfile` was missing; report v1 preserves that orchestration error.

The [live co-lifecycle report](../evidence/pc3-live-v100-root-on-rtx5080-v3-20261020.json) holds a newly allocated V100/R580 ROOT (`3251650399`) open while RTX5080/R595 requests that same numeric ID as `ROOT_CLIENT`. The target returned the requested ID, then its FREE and close succeeded; only afterward did the source receive the release token and successfully FREE its still-live ROOT. Allocation/free transport returns and embedded statuses passed, the guest ran as unprivileged uid1000, both GPU identities and driver versions matched before/after, and temporary helpers/payloads were removed. No CUDA context was opened, no module was loaded, and no driver/VM/PCI state changed. Report v1 stopped before hardware because its staged path violated the existing writer's prefix rule; v2 reached hardware and both RM sides passed, but a local report validator looked for the guest exit code at the wrong JSON level; v3 reran with the corrected predicate. This demonstrates simultaneous ROOT lifetimes across separate R580 guest and R595 host RM servers, not a same-Linux CUDA process, the proposed same-version R580 Open/proprietary pair, private sessions, UVM, P2P, or kernel execution.

The [reverse live report](../evidence/pc3-live-rtx5080-root-on-v100-v1-20261020.json) held RTX5080/R595 ROOT `3251634269` while the V100/R580 guest requested that ID. The guest returned it and passed its FREE before the RTX source received `release
`; the source FREE and both control closes passed. Host and guest identities were unchanged, all helpers and the payload were removed, and the execution-scope flags confirm no driver, module, VM or PCI changes. Together the two directions establish live numeric-ID coexistence for the installed R580/R595 RM servers. They do not establish shared Linux device files, private R580 backends, CUDA contexts, UVM or P2P.

Latest software gates are recorded in the current `docs/STATE.md` checkpoint. The parent CUDA13-Volta suite passed114 tests, and the pinned CUDA13 layout/runtime and mixed-fatbin static checks passed in `evidence/logs/software-verification-v20-parent-checks-20261020.log`. Earlier runs and their orchestration/default-probe failures are retained separately. These software checks do not upgrade the hardware qualification level.

## Typed GPU metadata routes (2026-10-21)

Fresh backend builds compile the bounded `GPU_GET_ID_INFO_V2` and `GPU_GET_ATTACHED_IDS` helpers against both private R580 sessions: [Open](../evidence/private-open-attached-ids-v1-20261021.json), module SHA-256 `1e40789b382d7323e57f89c77f1a2dfb3d3e852076d8d4bf09f809c7337149ac` (109 exports), and [proprietary](../evidence/private-proprietary-attached-ids-v2-20261021.json), module SHA-256 `b007d119f24eaa094d13282fd6262b7d986ffc2a5e1dc53da9cab1aadee8d36c` (111 exports, 303 imports, zero GPL-only). The [frontend](../evidence/frontend-attached-ids-v1-20261021.json) compiles with 28 exact backend imports and no unresolved or duplicate providers, module SHA-256 `54a81ab3169f149aa2d545e40d510447c389a565a2d4fdab3a48cc0b65255339`.

`GPU_GET_ID_INFO_V2` maps one synthetic frontend ID to the owning backend and restores that frontend ID in the returned record. `GPU_GET_ATTACHED_IDS` queries both fixed 32-entry RM tables, rejects noncontiguous, zero, duplicate, stale or catalog-inconsistent backend IDs, then emits frontend IDs in BDF order; a combined result over the public capacity fails rather than truncating. The CPU harness covers direct/XFER forms, backend transport and embedded failures, malformed IDs, copy faults and capacity. Backend control-session modeling executes 95 scenarios.

The observer binary was built twice from the same source and pinned R580 SDK headers with identical SHA-256 `e9f24e31e6b00035eb9515420e3e6d0f4ab77bbcd64e627dabcfe87e760e2157` (84,024 bytes). The refreshed build report hashes the complete 205-file compiler dependency closure and is [here](../evidence/rm-ioctl-observer-attached-ids-build-v2-20261021.json), SHA-256 `35ad846ced137e33e91c88fd8d64c25a55d50553c0bee20856f3a6d313689cf2`; every dependency was present and matched its recorded digest at verification time. CPU tests cover direct and XFER ioctl envelopes, bad pointers, exact nested-read scope, and summary/report consistency.

Installed-driver hardware captures now validate the real metadata payloads on both PC3 paths. The [RTX5080/R595 report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-attached-ids-observation-v3-20261021.json) records two successful `GPU_GET_ID_INFO_V2` calls returning ID512 and two successful `GPU_GET_ATTACHED_IDS` calls returning ID512 followed by `0xffffffff` sentinels. Its SM120 architecture kernel, five sync/async lengths and cleanup pass; probe exit77 correctly reports missing SM70. The [V100 guest/R580 report](../evidence/pc3-v100-gpu-id-and-attached-ids-observation-v2-20261021.json) records both commands returning ID5, two observations of each, and an SM70 numerical pass at all five lengths with cleanup and memory returning to0MiB. Both reports are mode0600, pin observer/probe/fatbin hashes, and record no experimental driver, module load, VM operation or PCI binding change.

This confirms the payload shapes and that each installed branch consistently uses its own GPU ID. ## Observed V100 `NV50_THIRD_PARTY_P2P` controls and experimental slice (2026-10-21)

The installed V100/R580 observer captured successful fixed-header/payload snapshots for VA-space registration (`0x503c0102`, 16 bytes), local-video-memory registration (`0x503c0104`, 32 bytes), and video-memory unregister (`0x503c0105`, 4 bytes). The pinned interpretation and limits are in [the P2P necessity audit](../evidence/r580-third-party-p2p-necessity-audit-v1-20261030.json). The legacy proprietary R580 wrapper adds four private, typed bridges around the native kernel RM control entry; it uses no userspace pointer. The frontend accepts those four controls only for tracked R580 `NV50_THIRD_PARTY_P2P` objects and tracked same-device legacy VASpace/local-user memory. It supports direct RM_CONTROL and XFER, validates fixed structure sizes and video-memory overflow/alignment, blocks resource FREE until registrations are successfully removed, and quarantines on ambiguous transport or output-copy failure. PID registration remains denied. The Open/Blackwell backend exports no such bridge and is never selected for these requests.

## `NVOS64` outer size and class-specific allocation copies (2026-10-21)

The paired V100/R580 and RTX5080/R595 captures show a successful `NV2081_BINAPI` allocation with `paramsSize=0`, a present `pAllocParms`, and a stable four-byte reserved-zero class payload. This is the actual caller shape, not a synthetic malformed packet. The [v2 paired comparison](../evidence/nv2081-binapi-allocation-comparison-v2-20261021.json), SHA-256 `ddda5cb47c8cbac129103970c6f9ef463a13fcebe233963e503fcfe494e59928`, now records that outer field alongside pointer presence and typed payload facts. Both sides have zero differences; `same_kernel_hybrid_validated` remains false.

Pinned Open R580 source forwards the outer field through `_nv04AllocWithAccess()`, then `serverAllocApiCopyIn()` derives the nested parameter size from the class resource descriptor. `rmapiGetClassAllocParamSize()` sets the registered allocation structure size and optional-null behavior; the `NV2081_BINAPI` entry uses optional `NV2081_ALLOC_PARAMETERS`. Thus the frontend's safety boundary is: accept only known outer forms for the class, copy a non-null payload at the exact SDK/class metadata size, use zeroed local storage only for the explicitly optional null form, and return a nested `copy_from_user` failure before backend dispatch. This source claim is specific to Open R580; stock hardware traces independently establish the caller shape on R580 and R595.

A strict `paramsSize`/pointer-pairing candidate was compiled and its CPU suite passed, but the subsequent direct capture scan falsified its premise. Its historical build report [`frontend-binapi-size-pair-v1`](../evidence/frontend-binapi-size-pair-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `666742d941f5408d507136aef8967c9d6d2fc99d9f959edea7aae6d7912a4dc1`, module SHA-256 `a7ad79c38b3e2826eaefbc7e2a40a04e534efe9289b1ef7db1486df9f3e8baef`, and test log are retained as a **rejected, never-loaded experiment**, not current validation. The current CPU lifecycle regression verifies the observed size-zero/pointer-present class copy, safe failure on an inaccessible nested pointer, optional absence, ownership, duplicate handles, and complete child cleanup. No hybrid module has been installed or loaded; same-kernel CUDA and hardware execution remain unproven.

The corrected frontend was rebuilt for kernel `7.0.0-38-generic` against the API-matched R580 backends. Report [`frontend-nvos64-class-size-v1`](../evidence/frontend-nvos64-class-size-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `cd2cad9c9465b9781f169c421775754351025ff45ff7656a5bf2b9b319caf581`; module SHA-256 `d5f3bdf599cc9b2fd13d8157a3d1adcc21ca06a42e23a90a7766d2ee41dde5f4`. All65 backend imports resolved with none unresolved or duplicated. The only build warning is a `pahole` version mismatch. Build inputs remained unchanged; no module load, installation, device registration, GPU access, or real RM call occurred. The full pinned suite passes **495 tests, zero skipped in 32.219s**: log `software-verification-nvos64-class-size-v1` (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-nvos64-class-size-v1-20261021.log`), SHA-256 `d007f3432fb713a88e94828fcd1074b5a11c9c2b0ec8be5df54949cd60b62df8`, mode0600. This is compile/CPU verification, not same-Linux hybrid qualification.

## Retained-channel references and duplicated UVM address spaces (2026-10-21)

Pinned R580.178.04 `nv_uvm_interface.h` documents `RetainChannel` as refcounted state requiring one `ReleaseChannel` per successful retain. `uvm_user_channel.c` stores one returned handle on the user-channel object and releases it after its fault tracker has drained; a second validation call retains and releases within one operation. The frontend's retained-channel identity now increments on a repeated same-pointer/same-parent/same-provider retain, calls the provider release on every release, and removes the identity only after the final one. The CPU/UBSan fixture checks those transitions, integer overflow, parent/provider conflicts, and that the parent remains live after the first release.

The R580 interface header describes `DupAddressSpace` as duplicating a user VA space into the kernel RM client so RM refcounts the VA-space object. Open R595 UVM calls it from `uvm_va_space.c` when constructing a GPU VA space. The frontend now selects the backend using the tracked `uvmGpuDeviceHandle`, calls only that RM, stages the output info, and registers the new opaque VA-space identity under the frontend device. Failed calls with non-null outputs and live pointer collisions are quarantined; `AddressSpaceDestroy` removes the dependency and the existing `DeviceDestroy` guard cannot retire the parent first.

The Linux `7.0.0-38-generic` build report [`hybrid-uvm-dup-address-space-v1`](../evidence/hybrid-uvm-dup-address-space-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `d4d34d130e01f63df517be9a5c636b5e1a50452cc19c966ce3617f3304a78052`, contains a2,070,944-byte module with SHA-256 `493806a32ea30d6091ba206aeb10162ab6fdee8d705321219dcccb9b7cf7c80b`. Its 137 exact backend imports resolve with no duplicate providers; `DupAddressSpace` and the other address-space export CRCs match R580. Build inputs remained unchanged; objtool was active with no return/objtool warnings. A `pahole` version mismatch warning remains. The UVM static closure report [`hybrid-uvm-symbol-coverage-dup-address-space-v1`](../evidence/hybrid-uvm-symbol-coverage-dup-address-space-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `fa2bceadbf5d451fd65b87d5221f22103fdb0fed35e7a1ef263a027d7e917d1a`, finds35 of76 frontend standard exports and41 absent; both private backends have complete import/CRC closure.

The full pinned software suite passes **563 tests with zero failures or skips in31.378s**. Both `HYBRID_PROBE` and `HYBRID_CUDA_PROBE` were pinned to the phase-probe binary SHA-256 `c67b11fc27e16dd88a282464910aa5533a75a67547744ca39cc4c9c6bc52971e`. Log `software-verification-uvm-dup-address-space-v1` (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-uvm-dup-address-space-v1-20261021.log`), SHA-256 `324bb9d5def1240aadfa5a3f86c4b57c6984131e21d6eb5a56ea585c8edc4895`, mode0600.

These results are source, CPU/UBSan and build evidence only. No module load/install, real RM invocation, UVM allocation, same-Linux V100+Blackwell process, GPU kernel under the hybrid module, or comparative performance test occurred. The newer physical-memory and allocation duplication routes below supersede the previous next-step order.

## UVM duplicated memory and allocation routing (2026-10-21)

The R580 interface contract distinguishes `DupMemory` (duplicate a physical RM allocation handle) from `DupAllocation` (map the source allocation into another tracked VA space). `FreeDupedHandle` releases the physical duplicate under the same device/provider. The frontend now registers a duplicate memory handle as a typed Device child only after the owning RM returns it; it withholds partial results, rejects a wrong-device free before dispatch, and retains the ownership record after backend free failure so a retry remains possible. CPU/UBSan tests exercise Open and legacy routes, collision and partial-output quarantine, failed release retry, and parent lifetime.

Pinned R580 `nv_uvm_interface.h` says vidmem duplication across GPUs is unsupported. Open R595 `uvm_rm_mem_map_gpu()` currently asserts system-memory allocation for this path, then calls `DupAllocation` from the owner VA space to the target VA space. `DupAllocation` therefore resolves both public VA-space identities, selects a provider only if both are owned by the same RM, returns `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED` between providers, and publishes `dstAddress` only on backend success. This is necessary because the RM handles are private to their namespace; passing a VA-space handle to the other RM would violate ownership. CPU/UBSan tests cover same-provider success for both backends, cross-provider rejection before call, identical spaces, unknown destination, and backend partial-output failure.

The Linux `7.0.0-38-generic` frontend build is [report `hybrid-uvm-dup-allocation-v1`](../evidence/hybrid-uvm-dup-allocation-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `c266193c9806e26d375f5881e9d4bc138bd216368a2ca331899f15f4e6c834c5`, mode0600. Its module is2,128,264 bytes, SHA-256 `c52f74742f930a2c85c93b2e05ba5f09f50831445fe328717242f6361d84c806`. All143 expected private backend imports resolve exactly once, `DupAllocation` and all other checked standard API CRCs match both R580 exports, inputs remained unchanged, and active objtool emitted no return warnings. The existing pahole-version warning remains.

The R580 UVM static closure audit is [report `hybrid-uvm-symbol-coverage-dup-allocation-v1`](../evidence/hybrid-uvm-symbol-coverage-dup-allocation-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `4af4d9a1c51e14e35ed8689d2eb8af53bee51f06557ad817113ba56eb9773e9d`, mode0600. It finds **38/76** standard exports in the frontend and38 still absent. Both private providers have no unresolved imports or CRC mismatches. This ratio is an ABI inventory, not a CUDA coverage metric: the missing APIs include paging, PMA, fault/error paths and CSL features with distinct hardware and lifecycle requirements.

The full software suite passes **567 tests with zero failures or skips in33.491s**; `HYBRID_PROBE` and `HYBRID_CUDA_PROBE` were pinned to the phase-capable probe SHA-256 `c67b11fc27e16dd88a282464910aa5533a75a67547744ca39cc4c9c6bc52971e`. See log `software-verification-uvm-dup-allocation-v1` (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-uvm-dup-allocation-v1-20261021.log`), SHA-256 `91c21720b16345e11c8f2a651228a595c3e9ad0d5114563b25f202b4bffbb7ec`, mode0600. This remains source, CPU/UBSan and compile evidence; no experimental module was installed or loaded, no real RM path ran, and the two GPUs remain on separate kernels.

## UVM P2P discovery and object lifetime (2026-10-21)

The pinned R580 UVM peer-discovery flow calls `nvUvmInterfaceGetP2PCaps()` for already registered GPU pairs and skips automatic peer setup only when `p2pLink` is `UVM_LINK_TYPE_NONE` or PCIe. The frontend reports `NONE` for a mixed-provider pair; this is an unsupported cross-RM capability response, not a measured topology result. It does **not** allow the second provider's GPU to register in the current single-UVM design: in ordinary mode, initialization first maps every existing GPU's semaphore pages through `DupAllocation`, and the current bridge rejects cross-provider VA-space pairs before RM dispatch. [The pinned source audit](../evidence/r580-uvm-cross-provider-static-v2-20261023T204103Z.json) traces the failure propagation. The result is static; hardware admission remains untested.

When UVM explicitly creates a peer object, same-provider `P2pObjectCreate` routes through the common RM. Its native `NvHandle` is translated to an opaque frontend handle because the Open and legacy namespaces may reuse the same number. The registry remembers the provider, native logical session, and both device parents; `P2pObjectDestroy` translates back through that record. Device/session teardown is blocked until the P2P object is released. Partial backend handles, success-with-zero, and native-handle collision create a quarantine record for both parents rather than risking an RM destroy against an ambiguous handle. Mixed-provider peer-object creation returns `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED` without a provider call. CPU/UBSan coverage includes both providers, cross-provider isolation, public-handle disambiguation, wrong session, owner retention, native-handle collision, and partial-output quarantine.

## R580 isolated staged-migration preflight (2026-10-28)

Patch [`0010-reject-unsupported-isolated-staged-migrations.patch`](../patches/r580.178.04/uvm/0010-reject-unsupported-isolated-staged-migrations.patch), SHA-256 `24977b5437402c2661d5fb6770b8d04dd22dd1aa413b1e1d90053cf4d87c183c`, adds a fail-closed preflight to the pinned R580.178.04 UVM path. It intersects the requested migration page mask and VA-block region with resident pages on each GPU; if any requested source GPU is outside the destination's copy-access mask in isolated mode, UVM returns `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED` before dispatching any copy. The patch is part of the default isolated R580 build series.

## R580 PMM tracker/channel lifetime guard (2026-10-28)

Patch [`0013`](../patches/r580.178.04/uvm/0013-reject-foreign-pmm-trackers-in-isolated-mode.patch) rejects that case under the lock before the detached copy; it returns `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED` and preserves existing cleanup. ## R580 tracker guard compatibility with the selected R595 UVM base (2026-10-28)

A clean temporary replay of R580 patch `0007-reject-cross-gpu-tracker-dependencies.patch` against pinned R595.99.02 content (3,810 files, tree SHA-256 `6d4ae86fb03f3afac63007c3c13422b114c93f5ab6a271ccb3dde5fb2440c066`) produces three exact rejected hunks: `uvm_push.c`, `uvm_gpu.c`, and `uvm_gpu_semaphore.c`. No fuzz was allowed. The probe preserves all rejected text and source-file hashes in this mode-0600 [report](../evidence/r580-tracker-patch-r595-compatibility-v1-20261028T2030Z.json), SHA-256 `821c5d04933316485dfb049adbb840c87ba8deb427c6f058dabccd86e1b274ae`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/r580-tracker-patch-r595-compatibility-v1-20261028T2030Z.log`), SHA-256 `14e712d6e6e3ddf270cf7052564f29fcb6b2021c127fbce15686267121388d99`. The hybrid `nvUvmInterfaceAreGpuProvidersSame()` bridge takes the global callback mutex around provider-registry lookup. A lock-held R595 tracker check must use a pre-resolved immutable provider identity or a deliberately conservative isolated mode; it must not call this mutex-taking API while holding PMM/VA-block locks. The probe and its pinned-source regression test pass, and the full software suite passes **1,781/1,781**: [report](../evidence/software-verification-r595-tracker-port-audit-v1-20261028T2030Z.json), SHA-256 `662dcc7e8350dc0228a68e5e40ee1fdb697ad13aea8d74b8f2d1923045b46b05`.

This result is source compatibility evidence only. The active R595.99.02 UVM candidate has not received the R580 tracker guards, and no candidate module was compiled from this attempted replay, installed, or loaded. Same-process hybrid execution remains unvalidated.

## Explicit hash-pinned CUDA user library selection (2026-10-29)

The common probe now accepts `--libcuda FILE` and resolves/checks the file before opening CUDA. The acceptance runner exposes `--libcuda-override FILE --libcuda-sha256 HASH`; it refuses missing or mismatched pins before launching the probe, forwards the exact resolved file, requires the probe's loaded-library path to match, and verifies the file identity again after execution. A CPU fake-driver run selected an alternate shared library and reported its resolved path. Negative contracts cover a nonexistent path before `cuInit`, absent and incorrect hashes before probe launch, a different reported library path, and mutation of the pinned file during execution.

The v9 probe closes a blocking-input edge case: a FIFO passed as `--libcuda` is rejected as a non-regular file before CUDA initialization instead of hanging. The build manifest pins the updated host source and builder; the host binary is not linked directly to `libcuda`, and the fatbin contains SM70 and SM120 cubins. Four runner tests verify external bundle selection and secure evidence output; the official suite runner then passes **2,192 tests, zero failures or skips, in 140.786 s** with the self-contained archived bundle. [Official-run report](</home/pc1/Documents/Codex/2026-10-18-mandat-codex-projet-nvidia-hybrid-driver/evidence/software-verification-libcuda-override-v4-20261029/report.json>), SHA-256 `83331b0ab1f3751d9ab992c094c4adefdb9153d1d2237948750eaab17c6eb44b`; [private full log](</home/pc1/Documents/Codex/2026-10-18-mandat-codex-projet-nvidia-hybrid-driver/evidence/software-verification-libcuda-override-v4-20261029/logs/software-test.log>), SHA-256 `e9b2f9a95f950a4fc68f0f88762c2f64e29c16ab084cc62df0f436ecdd735490`. The earlier standalone v9 build/source evidence remains in [the v3 evidence directory](</home/pc1/Documents/Codex/2026-10-18-mandat-codex-projet-nvidia-hybrid-driver/evidence/software-verification-libcuda-override-v3-20261029/>); its self-contained build manifest SHA-256 is `54278e7e0b861bec637032eabf603a684ff59aea535d97406416258d492126cb` and probe SHA-256 is `5bf13c28b4b4e3bb15952d3c0f776a71d924b7120a8e73a940dfa8ccb8b6d259`.

This change improves experiment reproducibility only. The tests use CPU fixtures and do not show that R595 `libcuda` initializes a V100, that the candidate modules initialize either GPU, or that the same process can execute on both architectures. The current Tailscale peer state is offline, PC3 SSH times out, and all ten NAOS sessions on the current page report unreachable; no hardware call or candidate module load occurred in this increment.

## Report qualification and signature boundary correction (2026-10-29)

The pageable-memory pass state is now named `system-pageable-memory-report-contract-pass` through the direct runner and both remote wrappers. PC3 compatibility outcomes say `phase-report-contract-valid`, `enumeration-report-contract-valid`, or an explicit report-contract system-mismatch result; these labels describe validated report structure and do not claim independently attested CUDA execution. Historical UVM context captures remain immutable and are accepted by the route-coverage tool only when their two raw SHA-256 values match the fixed allowlist; output records `cuda_execution_validated: false`, keeps UVM payloads unobserved, and names the evidence as node-open traces.

`aggregate_cuda_performance_references.py` now refuses to aggregate until the project has a detached-signature verifier backed by a trusted public key. Self-reported booleans and even a caller-supplied fake attestation object cannot pass this gate. This deliberately leaves stock-driver performance aggregation unavailable pending a real attestation mechanism. A review found and fixed a second stale helper pin, `TRACE_AUDIT_TOOL_SHA256`; the regression now loads both pinned PC3 helpers. The same review identified that caller-supplied current-format UVM traces could be paired with an invented summary, so current-format captures now require a hard-coded exact-hash allowlist entry. The current allowlist is empty; only the two existing legacy captures pass.

Focused regressions pass **273/273**, then **28/28** for the review follow-ups. The latest pinned full suite passes **2,194/2,194**, zero failures/skips, 141.857 s, exit 0. [Report](</home/pc1/Documents/Codex/2026-10-18-mandat-codex-projet-nvidia-hybrid-driver/evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261029T185626Z.json>), SHA-256 `82c82e74d3088d95d16f2099b872358533d775e2d2169ec435009b60466481a1`; [private log](</home/pc1/Documents/Codex/2026-10-18-mandat-codex-projet-nvidia-hybrid-driver/evidence/logs/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261029T185626Z.log>), SHA-256 `9e422ec92ad850172e87207f1e425f1e4cab3afcf5569516eb1c250536268f03`. This is software-suite-only evidence: no GPU workload ran and no driver module was installed or loaded. PC3 remains unreachable, and same-process V100+Blackwell execution, UVM runtime, P2P, and performance remain unvalidated.
## `NV50_MEMORY_VIRTUAL` route and R580 proprietary build — 2026-11-01

## GT200_DEBUGGER allocation observer, CPU verification (2026-11-01 UTC)

## Composed dual-GPU frontend contract — CPU fixture only (2026-11-02)

`test_composed_dual_gpu_frontend_contract` runs the composed fixture with both PCI orderings and both R580/R595 frontend ABI builds. In each case, one process-scoped fake client traverses BDF-sorted `CARD_INFO`, merged `GET_DEVICE_IDS`, per-GPU `GET_ID_INFO_V2`, typed `NV01_DEVICE_0` allocations, Blackwell/Volta standard GPU-node opens, `REGISTER_FD` ownership, and cleanup. The reverse-order case puts the V100 at BDF `0000:02:00.0` and the RTX provider at `0000:81:00.0`; provider ownership and local IDs must still follow the catalog rather than a fixed architecture order. The test passes each returned `deviceInstance` into the matching allocation, checks the exact control-session pointer received by the backend, verifies the BDF supplied to each provider's GPU-session creator, and confirms both GPU sessions are released exactly once. Focused command: `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 PYTHONPATH=tests python3 -B -m unittest test_hybrid_frontend.HybridFrontendTests.test_composed_dual_gpu_frontend_contract` (1 unittest, 4 executable/PCI-order combinations, pass).

The complete pinned software suite then passed **2,451/2,451**, exit 0, in **171.180 seconds**, with identical before/after hashes for all 361 test inputs. [Report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261102T064613Z.json), SHA-256 `e14ef5b9ad5c5f3c526938dded82af95a2eb9af5e28e96e76490673767748fff`; log SHA-256 `17719a1b65252f57c4f047f050191e87f11bf8e17b2caa2e9ff7bc23a8497b7f`. The report records `qualification=software-suite-only`, `runtime_or_hardware_validated=false`, and `loaded_or_installed=false`. This fixture does not exercise `libcuda`, CUDA Runtime, a real GPU, UVM, or mixed-generation kernel execution.

A fault-seeking mutation changed the production catalog comparator from ascending to descending BDF order. All four composed subcases rejected the mutation at the `CARD_INFO` identity/order contract; the source was restored byte-for-byte (SHA-256 `70839bb35175e85fa319c80aa70407c6ce1f52890f26d170a5954baf0bc731b4`) and the four cases passed again. [Mutation receipt v2](../evidence/dual-gpu-catalog-order-mutation-v2-20261102.json) binds the final test-source and suite hashes. This is evidence that the fixture catches this ordering defect, not evidence for the broader CUDA ABI or hardware path.

## UVM control-FD close and reuse — CPU fixture only (2026-11-02)

The `uvm-owner-control-fd-lifetime` host scenario now pins a tracked memory owner through FD 50, closes that descriptor, and reuses 50 for another control file whose public client and memory IDs collide but whose backend memory handle differs. The original pin retains the first `struct file`; a new pin through the reused FD resolves the second control file and selects backend handle `0xdecafbad`. RM FREE remains blocked for both allocations until their respective pins are released, after which each exact backend object is freed once. The focused enclosing unittest passes. [Receipt](../evidence/uvm-control-fd-reuse-v1-20261102.json), SHA-256 `de211dfc8d3881ecf78c3775d6320d8a51ff2cc5d5a49a88fd1ac8e3f5d7ab72`.

The pinned full suite passes **2,451/2,451**, exit 0, in **169.671 s**, with no test-source or implementation-source changes during the run. [Report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261102T071416Z.json), SHA-256 `3aa18f28b384e0724d3c3795f49eed89e700a8c775e98c95c707bb346103b190`; log SHA-256 `4db7aee316fab166cdaeaf9d698b0e9fef06aa6afc7aa3e6d933e246dde68cad`. This remains CPU/software evidence: no module load, GPU, CUDA runtime, or hardware UVM test occurred. The fixture covers FD close/reuse after `fget` acquired the file reference; it does not define a caller's concurrent close/reuse race before the kernel resolves the numeric FD.

The exact `hybrid580_uvm_copy_caps_from_r580` helper and R580 legacy payload declarations are extracted from `src/hybrid580_uvm_abi_compat.h`. The public R595 `UvmGpuCopyEngineCaps` payload and 64-entry bound are stored as a notice-preserving excerpt from R595.112.07 commit `d8257d95ae5fed364163e4df51db66df8b1ee0d2`, source header SHA-256 `5515b06a13072584d4668251c2e31bbb724118c914691eb4abf1cd76c1b63e65`.

The fixture checks all legacy capability fields, the conservative false `scrub` value absent from R580, direct Open-provider values through slot 63, provider selection without cross-RM fallback, output clearing after partial-write failure, unknown-handle quarantine, and null-device failure without taking the lock. It builds with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror -fsanitize=undefined`. A deliberate mutation that sourced `sysmemRead` from `sysmemWrite` was caught after making the fake values asymmetric; the production header was restored byte-for-byte (SHA-256 `8effcf75194e52a946ec94a1e7befcbae9113bb99b05f5b9bac32210058ce809`). The adjacent UVM wrapper group passes **18/18**.

The complete pinned software suite passes **2,784/2,784** in **184.543 s**, exit 0, with **436** test inputs and **167** implementation inputs unchanged. Sanitized [receipt](../evidence/software-verification-public-copy-engine-caps-sanitized-v2-20261103.json), SHA-256 `b16bcec7169fac37aa5442a38ef0e5f79eb40dde89ec0b6062e68372b4810bb2`, records `software-suite-only`, `runtime_or_hardware_validated=false`, and `loaded_or_installed=false`. Detailed mode-0600 report and log remain local under ignored `evidence/logs/`. No real RM endpoint, GPU, UVM runtime, CUDA workload, or module load was exercised by this test.

The unprivileged PC3 inventory receipt at [`pc3-user-gpu-inventory-summary-v5`](../evidence/pc3-user-gpu-inventory-summary-v5-20261103T1919Z.json), SHA-256 `451efd2406b1f21f33254bd456c115823dd40eab9dd8604dba8f8106765249f7`, confirms host RTX 5080 / SM120 / R595.99.02 and V100 / GV100 assigned to `vfio-pci` with `pc3-v100-qemu.service` active. This was a read-only inventory only; no workload or VM/driver change occurred. Raw report SHA-256 `7dd840db2dae4395b89d385d5a359502783baf6d2c8ab47198493e3b060ad17e` remains mode 0600 under ignored `evidence/logs/`.
## Public UVM fault-info lifecycle and query wrappers — 2026-11-03 19:59 UTC

`tests.test_hybrid580_uvm_fault_info_wrappers` extracts the exact fault-info backend ops, status/device-owner helpers, coupled FaultInfo/CSL registry lifecycle helpers, and all six public fault-info/query wrappers from `src/hybrid580_uvm_callback_bridge.c`. The GNU11 fixture uses `-Wall -Wextra -Werror -fsanitize=undefined -fno-sanitize-recover=all`, the production device/info registries, and fake Open/legacy RM endpoints. It verifies owner-specific initialization and query routing, the embedded `replayable.cslCtx` registry identity, paired publication/removal, duplicate and wrong-owner refusal, failed-init cleanup, failed-destroy quarantine, no provider fallback, output initialization, invalid booleans, allocation failure, and lock balance. A deliberately misrouted CSL-identity mutation fails the executable assertion. The minimal `UvmGpuFaultInfo` test model pins size 184 and `cslCtx` offset 88 from the separate ABI census; it is not the full NVIDIA type definition. Focused test: **1/1**.

The full pinned CPU/software suite passes **2,785/2,785** in **184.91 s**, with **438** test inputs and **167** implementation inputs unchanged. The sanitized [suite receipt](../evidence/software-verification-public-fault-info-wrappers-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `007061184afa8346ef993fd1c63803bffc6d4bc5528c8ba971478a82a2b4f6f7`, is qualified `software-suite-only`. Raw report/log are mode `0600` and excluded from Git. Fake RM calls and host-side test layouts do not validate the kernel module, real RM, CUDA, UVM execution, GPU behavior, or hardware.
